-heavy hitters problem with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
and uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n\log^{4}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n\right)\right)
bits of space.

Proof.

For each integer
j\geq 0
and a fixed integer
i
, let
u_{i,j}
be the last round for which
Z_{i,j}
in
Algorithm
1
is defined.
As before, let
t_{i}
be the update time during which the counter
a
in
Algorithm
1
is first set to
i
for any integer
i>0
.
Let
t^{(b)}_{i}
be the first round such that the counter
b
in
Algorithm
1
is first set to
j
, and we use the convention that
t^{(0)}_{i}=t_{i}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
be the event that
X_{a}
is a
\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)
-approximation to
F_{2}(1,t_{a})
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
be the event that
F(1,u_{a,j})-F(1,t_{a,j})\leq\frac{1}{2^{\beta-j-3}}F(1,t_{a})
for each positive integer
j>1
.
By
Lemma
2.2
and
Lemma
2.3
, we have
\mathbf{Pr}\left[\mathcal{E}_{1}\wedge\mathcal{E}_{2}\right]\geq 1-\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\delta\log n}{\varepsilon}\right)
.

Suppose there exists
r\in[n]
such that
(f_{r})^{2}\geq\varepsilon^{2}F_{2}(1,m)
.
Then coordinate
r
must either be
\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32^{2}}
-heavy with respect to
F_{2}(1,t_{a})
or
\frac{2^{k}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}{32^{2}\beta^{2}}
-heavy with respect to
F_{2}(t_{a,k},t_{a,k-1})
for some integer
k\in[\beta]
, where we use the convention that
t_{a,0}=t_{a}
.
Hence
BPTree
with threshold
\frac{2^{k}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}{32^{2}\beta^{2}}
with respect to
F_{2}(t_{a,k},t_{a,k-1})
will detect that
r
is heavy.
Moreover, note that if coordinate
r
is only reported as heavy in an algorithm corresponding to the level
k
difference estimator, then
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)\cdot F_{2}
of the contribution of coordinate
r
towards the overall
F_{2}
still appears after
r
is reported as a heavy-hitter.
Thus by keeping track of the frequency of
r
after it is reported, we obtain an estimate of the frequency of
r
up to an additive
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\cdot L_{2}
.
Since this is also the accuracy parameter of
{\mathcal{A}}_{a,k}
, then the space complexity follows.
∎

4
Robust
F_{p}
Estimation for
0<p<2

In this section, we use the framework of
Section
2
to give an adversarially robust streaming algorithm for
F_{p}
moment estimation, where
p\in(0,2)
.
As before, we require an
F_{p}
strong tracker and an
F_{p}
difference estimator in order to apply
Theorem
2.5
.
Whereas the
F_{2}
difference estimator was straightforward from the inner product approximation via
F_{2}
approximation property, it is not clear that such a formulation exists for
F_{p}
approximation.
Instead, we use separate sketches for our
F_{p}
strong tracker and our
F_{p}
difference estimator.
We present these subroutines in this section.
Finally, we again optimize our algorithm beyond the guarantees of
Theorem
2.5
so that our final space guarantees in
Theorem
4.14
matches the best known
F_{p}
algorithm on insertion-only streams, up to
\poly\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
terms.

We first require the following definition for
p
-stable distributions, which will be integral to both our
F_{p}
strong tracker and our
F_{p}
difference estimator.

Definition 4.1

(
p
-stable distribution)
.

[
Zol89
]

For
0<p\leq 2
, there exists a probability distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
called the
p
-stable distribution so that for any positive integer
n
with
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
and vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then
\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}x_{i}\sim\|x\|_{p}Z
for
Z\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
.

The probability density function
f_{X}
of a
p
-stable random variable
X
satisfies
f_{X}(x)=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{1+|x|^{1+p}}\right)
for
p<2
, while the normal distribution corresponds to
p=2
.
Moreover,
[
Nol03
]
details standard methods for generating
p
-stable random variables by taking
\theta
uniformly at random from the interval
\left[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\right]
,
r
uniformly at random from the interval
[0,1]
, and setting

X=f(r,\theta)=\frac{\sin(p\theta)}{\cos^{1/p}(\theta)}\cdot\left(\frac{\cos(\theta(1-p))}{\log\frac{1}{r}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}-1}.

These
p
-stable random variables are crucial to obtaining a strong
F_{p}
tracking algorithm.

Theorem 4.2

(Oblivious
F_{p}
strong tracking for
0<p<2
)
.

[
BDN17
]

For
0<p<2
, there exists an insertion-only streaming algorithm
{\textsc{PStable}}(1,t,\varepsilon,\delta)
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
bits of space and provides
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-strong
F_{p}
tracking.

Unfortunately,
PStable
is based on the
p
-stable sketch of
[
Ind06
]
, which offers a conceptual promise for the existence of the quantile estimators needed to guarantee provable bounds, but not an explicit computation.
Thus adapting the analysis of
PStable
in
[
Ind06
,
BDN17
]
for the purposes of our difference estimator seems to be a challenge.
Even for the case
p=1
, it does not seem evident how to adapt the median estimator of
PStable
to obtain a difference estimator for
F_{p}
.

Instead, we describe a formulation of Li’s geometric mean estimator
[
Li08
]
, which also provides a streaming algorithm for
F_{p}
, but was not previously known to offer strong tracking.
For a positive integer
q\geq 3
, let
d
be a multiple of
q
and let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
have independent
p
-stable random variables for the entries of
A
.
Then for a vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
y=Ax
, let
z_{i}:=C_{q,p}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}|y_{j}|^{p/q}\right)
be the geometric mean of the inner products of
q
random
p
-stable vectors with the vector
x
, where

C_{q,p}=\left[\frac{2}{\pi}\cdot\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{q}\right)\cdot\Gamma\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)\cdot\sin\left(\frac{\pi p}{2q}\right)\right]^{-q}.

We have the following characterization of the asymptotic behavior of
C_{q,p}
:

Observation 4.3

(Characterization of
C_{q,p}
)
.

[
Li08
]

C_{q,p}=\mathcal{O}\left(\exp(\gamma_{e}(q-1))\right)
, for the Euler-Mascheroni constant
\gamma_{e}\approx 0.57721
.

The value of
C_{q,p}
is chosen so that each
z_{i}
is an unbiased estimate of
\|x\|_{p}^{p}
, as a result of the following statement.
We can then bound the expectation and the variance of each random variable.

Lemma 4.4

(Expectation and variance of Li’s geometric mean estimator, Lemma 2.2 in
[
Li08
]
)
.

Let
d
be an integer multiple of an integer
q\geq 3
and let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
have independent
p
-stable random variables for the entries of
A
.
For a vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
y=Ax
, let
z_{i}:=C_{q,p}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}|y_{j}|^{p/q}\right)
be the geometric mean of the inner products of
q
random
p
-stable vectors with the vector
x
.
Then
\mathbb{E}\left[z_{i}\right]=\|x\|_{p}^{p}
and for
\xi:=\frac{C_{\frac{q}{2},p}}{C_{q,p}}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[z_{i}^{2}\right]\leq\left(\xi^{2}-1\right)\cdot\|x\|_{p}^{2p}
.

Thus by taking the arithmetic mean of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
variables
z_{i}
, we obtain a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
\|x\|_{p}^{p}
with constant probability by Chebyshev’s inequality.

4.1
F_{p}
Difference Estimator for
0<p<2

We now describe our
F_{p}
difference estimator and give the high-level details of the analysis. We use Li’s geometric mean estimator to maintain
A(v+w_{t})
and
Av
, where
A
is the sketching matrix for Li’s geometric mean estimator, and
v
and
w_{t}
are frequency vectors.
Observe that if we computed
A(v+w_{t})-Av
, then we would obtain
A(w_{t})
, which is a sketch that allows us to recover
F_{p}(w_{t})
.
However, we want to estimate
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(v)
rather than
F_{p}(w_{t})
.
Instead, we use the sketches
A(v+w_{t})
and
Av
to compute terms
z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2},\ldots
, where each
z_{i}
is the geometric mean of
q
consecutive entries in
A(v+w_{t})
and similarly
z^{\prime}_{i}
is the geometric mean of
q
consecutive entries in
Av
.
Since
z_{i}
is an unbiased estimator of
F_{p}(v+w_{t})
and
z_{i}
is an unbiased estimator of
F_{p}(v)
, it follows that
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
is an unbiased estimator of
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(v)
.
We take the average of the values
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
across
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
indices of
i
to obtain a single estimate and take the median of all estimates.
The challenge is achieving both the variance bounds on
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
while also obtaining a strong tracking property.
To bound the variance, we expand
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
to be a sum of
2^{q}-1
geometric means of
q
terms, each with at least one term
(\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}
.
Since
A_{j}
is a vector of
p
-stable entries, then
(\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}
has the same distribution as
(\|w_{t}\|_{p}\cdot X)^{p/q}
for a
p
-stable random variable
X
.
We also have
F_{p}(w_{t})=\gamma\cdot F(v)
, where
\gamma
is bounded by some absolute constant.
Thus we can bound the probability that
(\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}\geq\|v\|^{p/q}_{p}
.

(1)

Let
A
be a
d\times n
random matrix whose entries are i.i.d from the
p
-stable distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
, for
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n\right)\right)

(2)

For a parameter
q=3
, let each
z_{i}=\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}(Av+Aw_{t})_{j}^{p/q}
and
z^{\prime}_{i}=\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}(Av)_{j}^{p/q}
.

(3)

Output the arithmetic mean of
(z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}),(z_{2}-z^{\prime}_{2}),\ldots,(z_{d/q}-z^{\prime}_{d/q})
.

Fig. 7
:
Difference estimator for
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(w_{t})
with
0<p<2

Lemma 4.5

(Expectation and variance of difference estimator terms)
.

Let
p\in(0,2)
and
{\mathcal{S}}
be an oblivious stream on which
v
is the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and let
u
is the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{2}
.
Suppose
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
and
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
.
Then for each
i\in[d/q]
,
z_{i}
, and
z^{\prime}_{i}
as defined in
Figure
7
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}\right]=F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(u)
and

\Var(z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i})\leq 2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}=2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}(F_{p}(v))^{2}.

Proof.

For an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
, let
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and let
u
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{2}
so that
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
and
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
.
For any integer
d>0
, let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
be a matrix whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from the
p
-stable distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
.

Recall that Li’s geometric mean estimator first takes a geometric mean
z_{i}
of
q
separate inner products, where
i\in\left[\frac{d}{q}\right]
.
Explicitly, each variable
z_{i}
in the estimator takes the form

\displaystyle z_{i}:

\displaystyle=C_{q,p}\cdot\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v+u\rangle\right)^{p/q}

\displaystyle=C_{q,p}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(1+\frac{\langle A_{j},u\rangle}{\langle A_{j},v\rangle}\right)^{p/q}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v\rangle\right)^{p/q}\right),

where
A_{j}
is the
j
-th column of
A
.
We define

T_{i}:=\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(1+\frac{\langle A_{j},u\rangle}{\langle A_{j},v\rangle}\right)^{p/q}.

Similarly, Li’s geometric mean estimator for
v
is a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation for
\|v\|_{p}^{p}=F_{p}(v)
and each variable
z^{\prime}_{i}
in the estimator takes the form

z^{\prime}_{i}:=C_{q,p}\cdot\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v\rangle\right)^{p/q}.

By
Lemma
4.4
,
z_{i}
and
z^{\prime}_{i}
are unbiased estimators for
F_{p}(v+u)
and
F_{p}(v)
respectively, so that
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
is an unbiased estimator for
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
,

\mathbb{E}\left[z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}\right]=F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v).

We can also expand
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
so that

z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}=C_{q,p}\cdot(T_{i}-1)\cdot\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v\rangle\right)^{p/q}.

To bound the variance of
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
, we first require the following structural inequality to handle the terms inside of the product of
T_{i}
:

Claim 4.6
.

For
a,b\geq 0
and
p\leq 2
, we have
(a+b)^{p/q}\leq a^{p/q}+2b^{p/q}
.

Proof.

For
0\leq a\leq b
and
p\leq 2\leq q
, we have

(a+b)^{p/q}\leq(2b)^{p/q}\leq 2b^{p/q}\leq a^{p/q}+2b^{p/q}.

For
0\leq b\leq a
and
p\leq 2\leq q
, we have from Bernoulli’s inequality that

(a+b)^{p/q}=a^{p/q}\left(1+\frac{b}{a}\right)^{p/q}\leq a^{p/q}\left(1+\frac{pb}{qa}\right)\leq a^{p/q}+b^{p/q}.

∎

By
Claim
4.6
, we have

T_{i}\leq\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(1+\left(\frac{\langle A_{j},u\rangle}{\langle A_{j},v\rangle}\right)^{p/q}\right).

Hence,
T_{i}-1
is a sum of
2^{q}-1
terms and similarly,
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
is a sum of
2^{q}-1
product of
q
terms, each with at least one term
(\langle A_{j},u\rangle)^{p/q}
.
For example, one term in
z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}
is

C_{q,p}(\langle A_{1},u\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{2},v\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{3},v\rangle)^{p/q}\ldots(\langle A_{q},v\rangle)^{p/q},

while another term is

C_{q,p}(\langle A_{1},v\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{2},u\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{3},v\rangle)^{p/q}\ldots(\langle A_{q},v\rangle)^{p/q}.

By
Lemma
4.4
, each of these terms has second moment at most
(\xi^{2}-1)\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}
for
\xi=\frac{C_{\frac{q}{2},p}}{C_{q,p}}
.
Thus the sum of the
2^{q}-1
terms has second moment at most
(2^{q})^{2}(\xi^{2}-1)\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}
, so that the variance of the sum is at most
\mathcal{O}\left(2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}\right)
.
That is,

\Var(z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i})\leq 2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}=2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}(F_{p}(v))^{2}.

∎

Corollary 4.7

(Pointwise
F_{p}
difference estimator for
0<p<2
)
.

Let
p\in(0,2)
,
\delta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
{\mathcal{S}}
be an oblivious stream on which
v
is the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and let
u
is the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{2}
.
Suppose
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
and
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
.
Then there exists an algorithm that uses a sketch of dimension
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
and outputs an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(u)
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

For each
i\in[d/q]
, with
z_{i}
, and
z^{\prime}_{i}
defined in
Figure
7
, we have from
Lemma
4.5
that
\mathbb{E}\left[z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}\right]=F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(u)
and

\Var(z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i})\leq 2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}=2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}(F_{p}(v))^{2}.

Thus by Chebyshev’s inequality, the arithmetic mean of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{2^{2q}\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
differences
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
suffices to obtain additive
\varepsilon F_{p}(v)
error of the difference
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.
The probability of success can then be boosted to
1-\delta
by taking the median of
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
such instances.
∎

To obtain the strong tracking property and avoid extra
\log n
factors, this argument needs to be interleaved with carefully chosen values of
w_{t}
and bounding the supremum of
(\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}
across all values of
w_{t}
, rather than considering each inner product separately and taking a union bound.
We split the stream into
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}}\right)
times
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots
between which the difference estimator increases by roughly
\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
and apply a union bound to argue correctness at these times
\{r_{i}\}
, incurring a
\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
term.
To bound the difference estimator between times
u_{i}
and
u_{i+1}
for a fixed
i
, we note that the difference estimator only increases by roughly
\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
from
u_{i}
to
u_{i+1}
, so that we still obtain a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(v)
with a
\varepsilon^{1-16q/p^{2}}
-approximation to the difference, for a frequency vector
w_{t}
induced by updates between
u_{i}
and
u_{i+1}
.
We then bound the probability that the supremum of the error between times
u_{i}
and
u_{i+1}
is bounded by
\varepsilon^{1-16q/p}
by applying chaining results from
[
BCIW16
,
BCI+17
,
BDN17
]
that bound the supremum of a random process.

We first require the following structural property that bounds the supremum of the inner product of a random process with a vector of independent
p
-stable random variables.

Lemma 4.8
.

[
BDN17
]

Let
x^{(1)},x^{(2)},\ldots,x^{(m)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfy
0\preceq x^{(1)}\preceq\ldots\preceq x^{(m)}
.
Let
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a vector of entries that are i.i.d. sampled from the
p
-stable random distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
.
Then for some constant
C_{p}
depending only on
p
, we have

\mathbf{Pr}\left[\underset{k\leq m}{\sup}|\langle Z,x^{(k)}\rangle|\geq\lambda\|x^{(m)}\|_{p}\right]\leq C_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda^{2p/(2+p)}}+n^{-1/p}\right).

Lemma 4.9

(
F_{p}
difference estimator for
0<p<2
)
.

For
0<p<2
, it suffices to use a sketching matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
of i.i.d. entries drawn from the
p
-stable distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
, with dimension

d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)

to obtain a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator for
F_{p}
.

Proof.

For an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
, let
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and let
u_{i}
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{2}+1
to the last time when

\max(F_{p}(u_{i}),F_{p}(v+u_{1}+\ldots+u_{i})-F_{p}(v+u_{1}+\ldots+u_{i-1}))\leq\frac{1}{2^{q^{2}}}\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(v),

for some fixed constant
q\geq 3
.
We first show that our difference estimator gives an
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+u_{i})-F_{p}(v)
for each
i=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}}\right)
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that we obtain a
(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))
-approximation to both
F_{p}(v+u_{i})
and
F_{p}(v)
for all
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}}\right)
vectors
u_{i}
.
By
Corollary
4.7
, we have
\mathbf{Pr}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\poly(\varepsilon,\delta)}{2}
for an instance that uses
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right)
rows in the sketching matrix
A
whose entries are i.i.d. sampled from the
p
-stable distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
.
We thus condition on the event
\mathcal{E}
and assume we have additive
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\cdot F(v)
approximations at all times
u_{i}
.
Let
w_{t}
be a frequency vector induced by updates from time
u_{i}
and
u_{i+1}
, so that
F_{p}(w_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2^{q^{2}}}\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
It then suffices to show that the output of the difference estimator of
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(v)
at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\cdot F_{p}(v)
for all values of
w_{t}
over the stream, since such a result proves that the difference estimator changes by at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\cdot F_{p}(v)
between times
u_{i}
and
u_{i+1}
and conditioning on
\mathcal{E}
, we already have additive
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\cdot F(v)
approximations at all times
u_{i}
.
The claim would then follow by monotonicity of
F_{p}
.

We use the same computation as in
Corollary
4.7
to reduce the differences of the geometric means.
That is, our difference estimator first takes a geometric mean
z_{i}
of
q
separate inner products, where
i\in\left[\frac{d}{q}\right]
, formed from the entries of
A(v+w_{t})
.
Explicitly, each variable
z_{i}
in the estimator takes the form

\displaystyle z_{i}:

\displaystyle=C_{q,p}\cdot\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v+w_{t}\rangle\right)^{p/q}

\displaystyle=C_{q,p}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(1+\frac{\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle}{\langle A_{j},v\rangle}\right)^{p/q}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v\rangle\right)^{p/q}\right),

where
A_{j}
is the
j
-th column of
A
.
Similarly, each variable
z^{\prime}_{i}
in the estimator formed from the entries of
Av
takes the form

z^{\prime}_{i}:=C_{q,p}\cdot\prod_{j=q(i-1)+1}^{qi}\left(\langle A_{j},v\rangle\right)^{p/q}.

As in
Corollary
4.7
we can write
z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}
as a sum of
2^{q}-1
geometric means of
q
terms, each with at least one term
(\langle A_{j},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}
, e.g., one term in
z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}
is

C_{q,p}(\langle A_{1},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{2},v\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{3},v\rangle)^{p/q}\ldots(\langle A_{q},v\rangle)^{p/q},

while another term is

C_{q,p}(\langle A_{1},v\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{2},w_{t}\rangle)^{p/q}(\langle A_{3},v\rangle)^{p/q}\ldots(\langle A_{q},v\rangle)^{p/q}.

Thus by
Lemma
4.8
with
\lambda=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{8/p}}
, we have

\mathbf{Pr}\left[\underset{t\leq t_{2}}{\sup}|\langle A_{i},w_{t}\rangle|\geq\lambda\|w_{t}\|_{p}\right]\leq C_{p}\left(\varepsilon^{16/(2+p)}+n^{-1/p}\right)

and similarly

\mathbf{Pr}\left[\underset{t\leq t_{2}}{\sup}|\langle A_{i},v\rangle|\geq\lambda\|v\|_{p}\right]\leq C_{p}\left(\varepsilon^{16/(2+p)}+n^{-1/p}\right).

Thus with probability at least
1-\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)
, none of the
2^{q}-1
terms exceeds

\lambda^{p}\|w_{t}\|_{p}^{p/q}\|v\|^{(q-1)p/q}_{p}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{8}}\|w_{t}\|_{p}^{p/q}\|v\|^{(q-1)p/q}_{p}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{8}}{2^{q}}\cdot F_{p}(v),

since
F_{p}(w_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2^{q^{2}}}\varepsilon^{64q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
Hence, the sum of the
2^{q}-1
terms is at most
\varepsilon^{8}\cdot F(v)
, with probability at least
1-\mathcal{O}\left(2^{q}\varepsilon^{4}\right)
.
In other words, some particular difference of geometric means is at most
\varepsilon^{8}\cdot F(v)
, with probability at least
1-\mathcal{O}\left(2^{q}\varepsilon^{4}\right)
.
Taking a union bound over
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
geometric means, then the difference estimator
F_{p}(v+w_{t})-F_{p}(v)
outputs a value that is at most
\varepsilon^{8}\cdot F(v)
, with constant probability, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
.
Finally, if we take the median of
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
such estimators, then we can increase the probability of success to at least
1-\delta
.
As previously noted, this shows that the difference estimator provides strong tracking over the entire stream.

Because each entry takes
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits of space, then for constant
q
and a sketch with
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
rows, the total space required is

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right),

since we require a rescaling of
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)}
to union bound over the
\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
times
\{u_{i}\}
.
∎

Derandomization of
p
-stable random variables.

To handle the generation and storage of the entries of
A
drawn i.i.d. from the
p
-stable distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{p}
, we first note the following derandomization.

Lemma 4.10

(Lemma 8 in
[
JW18
]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be any streaming algorithm that stores only a linear sketch
A\cdot f
on a stream vector
f\in\{-M,\ldots,M\}^{n}
for some
M=\poly(n)
, such that the entries of
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}
are i.i.d., and can be sampled using
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits.
Then for any fixed constant
c\geq 1
,
{\mathcal{A}}
can be implemented using a random matrix
A^{\prime}
using
\mathcal{O}\left(k\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\right)
bits of space, such that for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with entry-wise bit complexity of
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
,

|\mathbf{Pr}\left[A\cdot f=y\right]-\mathbf{Pr}\left[A^{\prime}\cdot f=y\right]|<n^{-ck}.

We cannot immediately apply
Lemma
4.10
because our difference estimator actually stores
A\cdot(v+w_{t})
and
A\cdot v
and requires the entries of
A
to be i.i.d. We thus require the following generalization:

Corollary 4.11
.

For a constant
q\geq 1
, let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{q}\in\{-M,\ldots,M\}^{n}
for some
M=\poly(n)
be vectors defined by a stream so that
f_{i}
is defined by the updates of the stream between given times
t_{i,1}
and
t_{i,2}
, for each
i\in[q]
.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be any streaming algorithm that stores linear sketches
A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q}
, such that the entries of
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}
are i.i.d. that can be sampled using
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits, and outputs
g(A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q})
for some composition function
g:\mathbb{R}^{q}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Then for any fixed constant
c\geq 1
,
{\mathcal{A}}
can be implemented using a random matrix
A^{\prime}
using
\mathcal{O}\left(k\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\right)
bits of space, such that for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with entry-wise bit complexity of
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
,

|\mathbf{Pr}\left[g(A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q})=y\right]-\mathbf{Pr}\left[g(A^{\prime}\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A^{\prime}\cdot f_{q})=y\right]|<n^{-ck}.

Proof.

We first assume without loss of generality that all entries of
A\cdot f_{i}
are integers bounded by
\poly(n)
for each
i\in[q]
, due to the bit complexity of
A
and
f_{i}
.
Let
w_{i}=A\cdot f_{i}
for each
i\in[q]
and suppose the maximum entry, denoted
\|A\|_{\infty}
, satisfies
\|A\|_{\infty}\leq n^{\alpha}
for some constant
\alpha
.
Let
N=M\cdot n^{\alpha}
so that
\|A\cdot f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq N
for all
i\in[q]
.
Define the vector
v=\sum_{i=1}^{q}N^{2i}w_{i}
so that
\|v\|_{\infty}<N^{\prime}
for
N^{\prime}=N^{3q}
.
Note that
N^{\prime}=\poly(n)
so that all entries of
v
can be stored in
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits.
Moreover, observe that each coordinate
v_{j}
with
j\in[n]
has a unique
N^{2}
-ary representation.
Thus if
v_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{q}N^{2i}w_{i,j}
where
w_{i,j}
represents the
j
-th coordinate of vector
w_{i}
, then the system
v_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{q}N^{2i}\alpha_{i}
constrained to the condition that
|\alpha_{i}|\leq N
for all
i\in[q]
has a unique solution.
Hence, the vectors
w_{i}=A\cdot f_{i}
can be computed from the vector
v
.

By
Lemma
4.10
, we can use a random matrix
A^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{O}\left(k\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\right)
bits of space and have for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with entry-wise bit complexity of
O(\log n)
, that:

|\mathbf{Pr}\left[A\cdot v=y\right]-\mathbf{Pr}\left[A^{\prime}\cdot v=y\right]|<n^{-ck}.

Since any
\mathcal{A}
that computes
A\cdot v
can compute the vectors
A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q}
by the above argument,
\mathcal{A}
can then compute the composition
g(A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q})
.
Hence it follows that

|\mathbf{Pr}\left[g(A\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A\cdot f_{q})=y\right]-\mathbf{Pr}\left[g(A^{\prime}\cdot f_{1},\ldots,A^{\prime}\cdot f_{q})\right]|<n^{-ck}.

∎

We now argue that the pseudorandom generator of
Corollary
4.11
suffices to derandomize the correctness guarantees of our difference estimator.

Lemma 4.12

(
F_{p}
difference estimator for
0<p<2
)
.

For
0<p<2
, there exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-
difference estimator
for
F_{p}
that uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right).

Proof.

The difference estimator for
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
is given an input splitting time
t_{1}
.
We then need to argue correctness over all possible stopping times
t
with
t>t_{1}
, such that
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
for
p<1
or
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
for
p\geq 1
.
By
Corollary
4.11
applied with
q=2
, there exists a pseudorandom generator that succeeds with high probability
1-\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
over a specific value of
t
.
Since the stream has length
m=\poly(n)
, by taking a union bound over all
\poly(n)
choice of the stopping times
t
, we have that the pseudorandom generator is also correct with high probability over all possible stopping times
t
.
Thus from
Lemma
4.9
, we obtain the following guarantees of our difference estimator.

In particular, we remark that the guarantees of the difference estimator only require that the marginal distribution is correct at all times, rather than the joint distribution is correct.
Recall that the difference estimator is used to choose a final stopping time for the purposes of the framework in
Theorem
2.5
when its output is sufficiently large.
Specifically, the framework takes a set of outputs
s_{t_{1}+1},\ldots,s_{t}
from the difference estimator over the course of the stream and chooses a final stopping time
t_{2}
based on the first output that exceeds a certain threshold
T
.
We emphasize that our argument does not rely on fooling the final stopping time
t_{2}
chosen by the framework, since it requires a conditional statement on the set
s_{t_{1}+1},\ldots,s_{t}
of outputs from the difference estimator not exceeding the threshold
T
.

For example, consider a scenario where the probability over the choice of independent
p
-stable random variables that
s_{t_{1}+1}
exceeds
T
is
\frac{1}{2}
and the probability that
s_{t_{1}+2}
exceeds
T
is
\frac{1}{2}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{Pr}\left[s_{t_{1}+1}\geq T\right]=\frac{1}{2}
and
\mathbf{Pr}\left[s_{t_{1}+2}\geq T\right]=\frac{1}{2}
.
However, suppose that conditioned on the event that
s_{t_{1}+1}<T
, we have that
s_{t_{1}+2}
cannot exceed
T
, so that
\mathbf{Pr}\left[s_{t_{1}+2}\geq T|s_{t_{1}+1}<T\right]=0
.
Then since
Theorem
2.5
chooses the first time that exceeds
T
, the framework can never choose
t_{1}+2
, i.e.,
\mathbf{Pr}\left[t_{2}=t_{1}+2\right]=0
.
Since our derandomization does not use independent
p
-stable random variables and only fools the marginal probabilities (of a pair of times
(t,t_{1})
for each
t>t_{1}
), we need not fool the joint distribution, and thus not fool the choice of stopping time.
Hence, it could be that
Theorem
2.5
using our derandomized difference estimator selects
t_{2}=t_{1}+2
.
On the other hand,
t_{1}+2
is still a valid stopping time if the difference estimator is correct at time
t_{1}+1
, and the output does not exceed
T
.

In other words, while the geometric mean estimator can be fooled by fooling a constant a number of half-space queries, we may need to condition on
\poly(n)
intermediate stream positions.
Instead, we note that the derandomization of our difference estimator provides the same correctness guarantees.
Thus, even though the difference estimator with the pseudorandom generator could induce a different distribution on the final stopping time, the final stopping time
t^{\prime}_{2}
output by our derandomized algorithm and the algorithm itself can be used in our framework because
t^{\prime}_{2}
corresponds to the first output of the algorithm that exceeds the threshold
T
. We also have correctness at all intermediate times between
t_{1}
and
t^{\prime}_{2}
.
∎

Bit complexity and rounding of
p
-stable random variables.

We remark that for each inner product in
Lemma
4.12
to be stored in
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits of space, then each randomly generated
p
-stable random variable must also be rounded to
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits of precision.
Due to the rounding, that each summand changes additively by
\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
, so that the total estimate also changes by an additive
\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
, so that the total error for the estimate of
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
is
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.
The additive
\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
can be absorbed into the
\varepsilon\cdot F(v)
term with a rescaling of
\varepsilon
unless
F_{p}(v)=0
.
However, in the case
F_{p}(v)=0
, then our estimator will output
0
anyway, so that the rounding of the
p
-stable random variables still gives an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F(v)
.

4.2
F_{p}
Estimation Algorithm

We now give an adversarially robust streaming algorithm for
F_{p}
moment estimation with
p\in(0,2)
by using
Theorem
2.5
.

Theorem 4.13
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in(0,2)
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation for
F_{p}
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n\right)\right)
bits of space and succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

Proof.

For
p\in(0,2)
,
F_{p}
is a monotonic function with
(\varepsilon,m)
-twist number
\lambda=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\right)
, by
Observation
1.12
.
By
Lemma
4.12
and
Theorem
4.2
, there exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator that uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2/p}\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)

and an oblivious strong tracker
PStable
for
F_{p}
that uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right).

Therefore by using the
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator and the oblivious strong tracker for
F_{p}
in the framework of
Algorithm
1
, then consider applying
Theorem
2.5
with parameters
C=2/p\geq 1
,
S_{1}(n,\delta,\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
, and
S_{2}=0
.
Namely,
Theorem
2.5
gives an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
for
F_{p}
that succeeds with constant probability and uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right).

∎

Optimized
F_{p}
Algorithm.

To improve the space dependency, we use the same technique as in
Section
3
.
That is, recall that the counter
a
in
Algorithm
1
tracks the active instances
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
output by the algorithm.
Then we only maintain the most sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,c}
for the smallest
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
values of
i
that are at least the value of the counter
a
, instead of maintaining
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
total sketches.
Because the output increases by a factor of
2
each time
a
increases, any larger index will have only missed
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)
fraction of the
F_{p}
of the stream and thus still output a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation.

Theorem 4.14

(Adversarially robust
F_{p}
streaming algorithm for
p\in(0,2)
)
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in(0,2)
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
for
F_{p}
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
bits of space and succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

Proof.

Using the above optimization, there are simultaneously
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active indices
a
and
\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)
active indices
c
corresponding to sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
.
Recall from
Theorem
2.5
that for fixed
a
and
C>1
, the total space for each
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,j}
across the
\beta
granularities is

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot S_{1}(n,\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot S_{2}(n,\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon)\right),

where for our purposes,
S_{1}(n,\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}+\log\log n\right)
and
S_{2}=0
for our
F_{p}
strong tracker and
F_{p}
difference estimator.
Since there are
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
total indices
a
over the course of the stream, then each sketch must have failure probability
\frac{\delta}{\poly\left(\log n,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)}
for the entire algorithm to have failure probability
\delta=\frac{2}{3}
.
However, since we maintain at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
simultaneous active values of
a
, then the
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
bits of space are required to perform the sketching stitching and granularity changing framework.
Since there are
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active indices of
a
, consisting of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
subroutines, then it takes
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
additional bits of space to store the splitting times for each of the
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active subroutines across a stream of length
m
, with
\log m=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
.
Hence, the total space required is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
.
∎

Similarly, we have:

Theorem 4.15

(Adversarially robust
F_{p}
streaming algorithm for
p\in(0,1)
)
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in(0,1]
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
for
F_{p}
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\right)
bits of space and succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

Finally, we remark that our analysis for
Lemma
4.12
can be repeated to show that Li’s geometric mean estimator provides strong
L_{p}
tracking for
p\in(0,2)
.
Namely by first union bounding over all
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
times in the stream where the value of
F_{p}
on the underlying frequency vector induced by the stream roughly doubles, we again reduce the problem to maintaining an accurate estimation of
F_{p}
between two times in which the value of
F_{p}
roughly doubles.
We can subsequently apply the techniques of
Lemma
4.9
by further breaking down the stream into times when the value of
F_{p}
increases by a factor of
(1+\poly(\varepsilon))
and bounding the same terms
z_{i}
, as defined in
Figure
7
.
This analysis incurs an extra factor of
\log\log n
due to an initial union bound over
\poly\left(\log n,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
times in the stream rather than
\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.
Hence, the space required to implement Li’s geometric mean estimator as a strong
L_{p}
tracker in the random oracle model is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
, which matches the strong
L_{p}
tracker of
[
BDN17
]
.
To derandomize the algorithm, we incur an additional
(\log\log n)^{2}
factor due to
Corollary
4.11
.

Theorem 4.16
.

For
p\in(0,2)
, there exists a one-pass streaming algorithm that uses total space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
bits and provides
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-strong tracking for the
F_{p}
moment estimation problem.

Proof.

Consider an instance of Li’s geometric mean estimator with
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)\right)
rows.
We define a sequence of times
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{w}
throughout an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
so that each time
t_{i}
is implicitly defined as the last time that
F_{p}(1:t_{i})\leq 2^{i-1}
and thus
w=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
.
For each
i\in[w]
, we further consider a sequence of times
t_{i,1},t_{i,2},\ldots
so that time
t_{i,j}
is defined as the last time
t
such that that

\max(F_{p}(t_{i,j-1}+1:t),F_{p}(1:t)-F_{p}(1:t_{i,j-1}))\leq\frac{1}{2^{q^{2}}}\varepsilon^{16q/p^{2}}\cdot F_{p}(1:t_{i}).

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
be the event that Li’s geometric mean estimator gives a
\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)
-approximation to
F_{p}(1:t_{i})
for all
i\in[w]
and let
\mathcal{E}_{2}
be the event that Li’s geometric mean estimator gives a
\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)
-approximation to
F_{p}(1:t_{i,j})
for all
t_{i,j}
.
Since there are
\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
times
\{t_{i}\}\cup\{t_{i,j}\}
, then we require
\delta^{\prime}=\delta/\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
to obtain success probability
1-\delta
after taking a union bound over
\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
times.
By
Lemma
4.4
, the arithmetic mean of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
variables
z_{i}
achieves a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(1:t)
with constant probability for a fixed time
t
.
Thus by taking the median of
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
such means and taking a union bound, we have that
\mathbf{Pr}\left[\mathcal{E}_{1}\wedge\mathcal{E}_{2}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}
.

For times
a,b\in[m]
, let
{\mathcal{L}}(a:b)
be the output of Li’s geometric mean estimator for the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from times
a
to
b
.
From the chaining argument in
Lemma
4.9
and a sketching matrix with dimension
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)\right)
rows, we have that for a fixed
i\in[w]
, there does not exist
t\in[t_{i,j}+1,t_{i,j+1}]
such that
|{\mathcal{L}}(1:t)-{\mathcal{L}}(1:t_{i,j}+1)|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}F_{p}(1:t_{i,j})
, with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
.
Hence conditioned on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{L}}(1:t)
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
approximation to
F_{p}(1:t)
for all times
t\in[t_{i,j}+1,t_{i,j+1}]
, with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
.
We again take a union bound over
\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
times
\{t_{i}\}\cup\{t_{i,j}\}
, so that setting
\delta^{\prime}=\delta/\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)
implies that the
{\mathcal{L}}(1:t)
simultaneously gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to all
F_{p}(1:t)
with probability
1-\delta
.
Hence, the total number of rows required in
D
is
d=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)\right)
, which translates to
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
total rows.
Each entry in the sketch uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits of space to store.
Moreover, we incur an additional
(\log\log n)^{2}
factor due to
Corollary
4.11
to derandomize the algorithm.
Therefore, the total space in bits is

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right).

∎

Applications to Entropy Estimation.

Finally, we give an application to adversarially robust entropy estimation, similar to
Theorem
8.15
.

Theorem 4.17

(Adversarially robust entropy streaming algorithm)
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs an additive
\varepsilon
-approximation to Shannon entropy and uses
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n\right)
bits of space and succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

Proof.

By
Observation
8.13
and
Lemma
8.14
, it suffices to obtain adversarially robust
(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})
-approximation algorithms to
F_{y_{i}}(v)
for all
y_{i}\in(0,2)
in the set
\{y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}\}
, where
k=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log m
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{12(k+1)^{3}\log m}
.
By
Theorem
4.14
with accuracy parameter
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we can obtain such robust algorithms approximating each
F_{y_{i}}(v)
, using space

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}\log n(\log\log n)^{2}\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right).

Moreover, the failure probability of each algorithm is
1-\poly\left(\varepsilon,\frac{1}{\log n}\right)
, due to the rescaling of the failure probability
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n\right)}
in both
Lemma
4.12
and
Theorem
4.2
.
Hence for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{12(k+1)^{3}\log m}
and
\log m=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
, each of the
\mathcal{O}\left(k\right)
algorithms use space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n\right)
.
Since
k=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log m
, the overall space complexity follows.
∎

5
Robust
F_{p}
Estimation for Integer
p>2

In this section, we use the framework of
Section
2
to give an adversarially robust streaming algorithm for
F_{p}
moment estimation where
p>2
is an integer.
We again require both an
F_{p}
strong tracker and an
F_{p}
difference estimator to use
Theorem
2.5
.
Recall that for integer
p>2
, the dominant space factor is
n^{1-2/p}
and thus we will not try to optimize the
\log n
factors.
Hence we obtain an
F_{p}
strong tracker by adapting an
F_{p}
streaming algorithm and a union bound over
m
points in the stream.
The main challenge of the section is to develop the
F_{p}
difference estimator, since we have the following
F_{p}
strong tracker:

Theorem 5.1

(
[
Gan11
]
, Theorem 22 in
[
GW18b
]
)
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists an insertion-only streaming algorithm
{\textsc{GHSS}}(1,t,\varepsilon,\delta)
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{2}n\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
bits of space that gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to the
F_{p}
moment.

By setting
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\poly(n)}
, we can apply
Theorem
5.1
across all
m=\poly(n)
times on a stream, thus obtaining a strong tracker.

Theorem 5.2

(Oblivious
F_{p}
strong tracking for integer
p>2
)
.

[
Gan11
,
GW18b
]

For integer
p>2
, there exists an insertion-only streaming algorithm
{\textsc{GHSS}}(1,t,\varepsilon,\delta)
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log\frac{n}{\delta}\log^{2}n\right)
bits of space and provides
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-strong
F_{p}
tracking.

To acquire intuition for our
F_{p}
estimation algorithm, we first recall the following definition of perfect
L_{p}
sampling.

Definition 5.3

(
L_{p}
sampling)
.

Let
f\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and failure probability
\delta\in(0,1)
.
A
perfect
L_{p}
sampler
is an algorithm that either outputs a failure symbol
\bot
with probability at most
\delta
or an index
i\in[n]
such that for each
j\in[n]
,

\mathbf{Pr}\left[i=j\right]=\frac{u_{j}^{p}}{F_{p}(u)}+\mathcal{O}\left(n^{-c}\right),

for some arbitrarily large constant
c\geq 1
.

We use the expansion
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\binom{p}{k}\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
for our difference estimator for
F_{p}
moment estimation for integer
p>2
, where
u^{k}
denotes the coordinate-wise
k
-th power of
u
.
Suppose we use a perfect
L_{p}
sampler to sample a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{v_{a}^{k}}{\|v\|_{k}^{k}}
.
We then set
Z
to be the
a
-th coordinate of the frequency vector
v^{p-k}
.
Observe that
u
arrives completely after the splitting time denotes the end of the updates to frequency vector
v
.
Thus we can sample
a
at the splitting time and then explicitly compute
Z
.
We can then obtain an unbiased estimate
Y
to
\|v\|_{k}^{k}
with low variance, so that the expected value of
YZ
would be exactly
\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
and moreover,
YZ
is a “good” approximation to
\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
.

The downfall of this approach is that it requires a perfect
L_{p}
-sampler for
p>2
, which is not known.
Perfect
L_{p}
-samplers are known for
p\leq 2

[
JW18
]
, but their constructions are based on duplicating each stream update
\poly(n)
times.
Hence adapting these constructions to build perfect
L_{p}
-samplers for
p>2
would be space-inefficient, since the space dependence is
\Omega(n^{1-2/p})
for
p>2
, rather than
\polylog(n)
for
p\leq 2
.
Thus after duplication the space required would be
\Omega((\poly(n))^{1-2/p})
rather than
\polylog(\poly(n))
.
Note that the former requires space larger than
n
while the latter remains
\polylog(n)
.
One possible approach would be to use approximate
L_{p}
samplers and their variants
[
MW10
,
JST11
,
AKO11
,
MRWZ20
]
, but these algorithms already have
\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}
space dependency for each instance, which prohibits using
\Omega\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
instances to reduce the variance of each sampler.
Instead, we use the following perfect
L_{2}
-sampler of
[
JW18
]
to return a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{v_{a}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}\pm\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.

Theorem 5.4

(Perfect
L_{2}
sampler)
.

[
JW18
]

Given failure probability
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a one-pass streaming algorithm
Sampler
that is a perfect
L_{2}
sampler and uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}n\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
bits of space.

We also obtain unbiased estimates
X
and
Y
of
v_{a}^{k-2}
and
\|v\|_{2}^{2}
, respectively.
Given
a\in[n]
, we then track the
a
-th coordinate of
u^{p-k}
exactly.
We show that the product of these terms
X
,
Y
, and
u^{p-k}
forms an unbiased estimate to
\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
.
We then analyze the variance and show that taking the mean of enough repetitions gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
.
By repeating the estimator for each summand in
\sum_{k=1}^{p}\binom{p}{k}\langle v^{k},u^{p-k}\rangle
, it follows that we obtain a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator for
F_{p}
.

We first require the well-known
CountSketch
algorithm for identifying heavy-hitters, which consists of a table with
\log\frac{n}{\delta}
rows, each consisting of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
buckets, to identify
\varepsilon\cdot L_{2}
heavy hitters.
For each row, each item
i\in[n]
in the universe is hashed to one of the
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
buckets along with a random sign.
The signed sum of all items assigned to each bucket across all rows is tracked by the data structure and the estimated frequency of each item
i
is the median of the values associated with each bucket that
i
is hashed to, across all rows.

(1)

Find a list
{\mathcal{H}}
that includes all
i\in[n]
with
v_{i}\geq\frac{\gamma^{1/p}}{16}\|v\|_{p}
.

(2)

Using
CountSketch
, obtain an estimate
\widehat{v_{i}}
to
v_{i}
with additive error
\frac{\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}}{64\gamma}\|v\|_{p}
for each
i\in{\mathcal{H}}
and let
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the vector such that
h_{i}=\widehat{v_{i}}
if
i\in{\mathcal{H}}
and zero otherwise.

(3)

Perform perfect
L_{2}
sampling on
v-h
to obtain a set
{\mathcal{S}}
of size
k=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
.

(4)

Obtain an estimate
\widehat{s_{i}}
to
v_{i}-h_{i}
for each
i\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

(5)

Let
W
be a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
\|v-h\|_{2}^{2}
.

(6)

Output
W+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\binom{p}{k}\left(\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}}\widehat{v_{i}}^{k},u_{i}^{p-k}+W\cdot\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}\widehat{s_{i}}^{k-2},u_{i}^{p-k}\right)
.

Fig. 8
:
F_{p}
difference estimator for
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
with integer
p>2
.

Lemma 5.5

(Moment estimation of sampled items)
.

For integer
p>2
and failure probability
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a one-pass streaming algorithm that outputs an index
i\in[n]
with probability
\frac{u_{i}^{2}}{F_{2}(u)}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
, as well as an unbiased estimate to
u_{i}^{p}
with variance
u_{i}^{2p}
.
The algorithm uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}n\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
bits of space.

Proof.

Recall that
Sampler
first duplicates each coordinate
u_{i}
for
i\in[n]
a total number of
n^{c}
times and for each
(i,j)\in[n]\times[n^{c}]
,
Sampler
scales
u_{i}
to obtain random variables
z_{i,j}=\frac{u_{i}}{\sqrt{e_{i,j}}}
for an exponential random variable
e_{i,j}
(the exponential random variables in
[
JW18
]
are then derandomized).
This linear transformation to the frequency vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
results in a vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{c+1}}
, which can also be interpreted as having dimensions
n\times n^{c}
.
Moreover,
Sampler
only outputs an index
i\in[n]
if there exists an index
j\in[n^{c}]
if
|z_{i,j}|\geq\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)|z|_{2}
.
We then run an instance of
CountSketch
with constant factor approximation to obtain an estimate
\widehat{z_{i,j}}
for the frequency of
z_{i,j}
and set
\widehat{u_{i}}=\sqrt{e_{i,j}}\cdot\widehat{z_{i,j}}
.
Recall that each estimate
\widehat{z_{i,j}}
of
z_{i,j}
is

\sum s_{i,j}{\mathbf{1}}_{h(a,b)=h(i,j)}s_{a,b}z_{a,b},

where
s_{a,b}
is a uniformly random sign and
{\mathbf{1}}_{h(a,b)=h(i,j)}
is the indicator variable of whether
h(a,b)=h(i,j)
.
That is, we define
{\mathbf{1}}_{h(a,b)=h(i,j)}=1
if
h(a,b)=h(i,j)
and
{\mathbf{1}}_{h(a,b)=h(i,j)}=0
if
h(a,b)\neq h(i,j)
.
Hence,
\widehat{z_{i,j}}
is an unbiased estimate of
z_{i,j}
so that
\widehat{u_{i}}
is an unbiased estimate of
u_{i}
.
Moreover, the variance of
\widehat{z_{i,j}}
is at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\|z\|_{2}^{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(z_{i,j}^{2}\right)
so that the variance of
\widehat{u_{i}}
is
\mathcal{O}\left(u_{i}^{2}\right)
.
Thus if we use
p
independent instances of
CountSketch
with estimates
\widehat{u_{i}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{u_{i}}^{(p)}
, their product is an unbiased estimate to
u_{i}^{p}
with variance
u_{i}^{2p}
, as desired.

Each of the
p
instances of
CountSketch
with constant factor approximation uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
bits of space.
Since the perfect
L_{2}

Sampler
uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}n\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
bits of space by
Theorem
5.4
, then the total space used is
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}n\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
.
∎

Unfortunately, perfect
L_{2}
sampling coordinates of
v
alone is not enough; the variance of the resulting procedure is too high to obtain space dependency
\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}
.
Thus we also run a subroutine that removes a set of “heavy” coordinates
{\mathcal{H}}
of
v
and tracks the corresponding coordinates of
u
.
Although we have the exact values of
u_{a}
for
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
, we still do not have exact values of
v_{a}
; instead, we have estimates
\widehat{v_{a}}
for each
v_{a}
with
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
.
Setting
h
to be the sparse vector that contains the estimates
\widehat{v_{a}}
for each
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
and
w:=v-h
, our algorithm perfect
L_{2}
samples from
L_{2}
sample from.
To show correctness, we decompose

F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}+\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}.

The heavy-hitter subroutine allows accurate estimation to the first term and the perfect
L_{2}
sampling subroutines allows accurate estimation to the second term.
Finally, we remark that perfect
L_{2}
sampling incurs a term of
v_{a}^{2}
.
Thus we further need to split the estimation of the second term into the cases where
i=1
and
i>1
.

Estimation of
\langle v^{i},u^{p-i}\rangle
for
i>1
.

We first show that our difference estimator gives an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}
with
i\geq 2

Lemma 5.6
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists an algorithm that uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
and outputs an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}
with
i\geq 2
.

Proof.

For an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
, let
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and
u
be the frequency vector induced by updates from time
t_{2}
to
t
exclusive, with
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
By
Theorem
3.10
, there exists an algorithm
HeavyHitters
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2-2/p}\log^{2}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
space and outputs a list
{\mathcal{H}}
of coordinates and estimates
\widehat{v_{i}}
with additive error
\frac{\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}}{64\gamma}\|v\|_{p}
.
Moreover,
{\mathcal{H}}
includes all coordinates
i\in[n]
such that
v_{i}\geq\frac{\gamma^{1/p}}{16}\|v\|_{p}
and no coordinate
j
such that
v_{j}\leq\frac{\gamma^{1/p}}{32}\|v\|_{p}
.
Let
h
be the vector consisting of the estimates
\widehat{v_{i}}
for each
i\in[n]
with
i\in{\mathcal{H}}
and
0
in the positions
i
for which
i\notin{\mathcal{H}}
.
We define the vector
w:=v-h
.

We use
Sampler
to sample indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n]
with so that each sample is a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.
We obtain unbiased estimates to
\widehat{w_{j_{1}}^{p-i-2}},\ldots,\widehat{w_{j_{k}}^{p-i-2}}
of
w_{j_{1}}^{p-i-2},\ldots,w_{j_{k}}^{p-i-2}
through
Lemma
5.5
.
We also obtain a
(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))
-approximation unbiased estimate
W
of
\|w\|_{2}^{2}
.
Thus for each
b\in[k]
, the product
\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{p-i-2}}\cdot W\cdot u_{j_{b}}^{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{p-i-2}}\cdot W\cdot u_{j_{b}}^{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\langle\widehat{v_{a}}^{p-i},u_{a}^{i}\rangle+\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{p-i-2}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot u_{a}^{i}

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\in[n]\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{p-i-2}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot u_{a}^{i}.

Observe that since
\widehat{v_{a}}
is a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
v_{a}
for
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
, then
|w_{a}|\leq\varepsilon|v_{a}|
, so that the second summation is at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\langle v^{p-i},u^{i}\rangle
.
Moreover, we have
w_{a}=v_{a}
for
a\in[n]\setminus{\mathcal{H}}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{p-i-2}}\cdot W\cdot u_{j_{b}}^{i}\right]

\displaystyle\in(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))|\langle w^{p-i},u^{i}\rangle|+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}.

Similarly, the variance is at most

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{p-i-2}}\cdot W\cdot u_{j_{b}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[n]}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{2p-2i-4}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{4}\cdot u_{a}^{2i}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[n]}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))w_{a}^{2p-2i-2}\cdot\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot u_{a}^{2i}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[n]}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))w_{a}^{2p-4}\cdot\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot u_{a}^{2}.

By Hölder’s inequality, the variance is at most

\displaystyle(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{a\in[n]}w_{a}^{2p-4}u_{a}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\left(\sum_{a\in[n]}u_{a}^{p}\right)^{2/p}\left(\sum_{a\in[n]}w_{a}^{2p}\right)^{1-2/p}

\displaystyle=(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|u\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|w\|_{2p}^{2p-4}.

Since
(1+\varepsilon)\|w\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|v\|_{2}^{2}
for
\varepsilon\leq 1
and
\|u\|_{p}^{p}\leq\gamma\|v\|_{p}^{p}
, then the variance is at most

\displaystyle 2\|v\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|w\|_{2p}^{2p-4}.

Recall that the vector
w
is formed by removing from
v
the coordinates
i\in[n]
such that
v_{i}\geq\frac{\gamma^{1/p}}{16}\|v\|_{p}
.
Thus,
|w_{i}|\leq\frac{\gamma^{1/p}}{16}\|v\|_{p}
for all
i\in[n]
.
Subject to these constraints, we have that

\|w\|_{2p}^{2p}\leq\frac{16^{p}}{\gamma}\cdot\frac{\gamma^{2}}{16^{2p}}\|v\|_{p}^{2p}.

Thus we have

\|w\|_{2p}^{2p-4}\leq\frac{\gamma^{1-2/p}}{16^{p-2}}\|v\|_{p}^{2p-4}.

Hence, the variance is at most

\displaystyle\|v\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\gamma^{2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\gamma^{1-2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2p-4}\leq\frac{\gamma}{n^{1-2/p}}\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|v\|_{p}^{2p-4}.

Thus by setting
k=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
, we obtain an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
with constant probability.
We can then boost this probability to
1-\frac{\delta}{\poly(n)}
by repeating
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
times.
Hence, we have a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator for
F_{p}
.

To analyze the total space complexity, first observe that we use
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log n\right)
instances of
Sampler
,
CountSketch
, and an
F_{2}
moment estimation algorithm.
For
CountSketch
, we only require constant factor approximation, so that each instance uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}n\right)
.
Similarly for an
F_{2}
moment estimation algorithm, we only require constant factor approximation, so that
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}n\right)
space suffices, by
Theorem
3.8
.
Each instance of
Sampler
uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}n\right)
.
Hence, the total space is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
.
∎

Estimation of
\langle v,u^{p-1}\rangle
.

We first duplicate each coordinate of
u
and
v
a total number of
M=n^{c}
times, for some sufficiently large constant
c>0
.
For each
i\in[n]
and
j\in[M]
, we scale
u_{i}
and
v_{i}
by
1/e_{i,j}^{1/2}
, where
e_{i,j}
is a random exponential variable truncated at
N^{2}
, where
N=nM
.
Thus we have that the cdf of
\frac{1}{e_{i,j}}
satisfies
\mathbf{Pr}\left[\frac{1}{e_{i,j}}\geq x\right]\leq\Theta\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)
for
x>0
, so that
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{e_{i,j}}\right]=\int_{0}^{N^{2}}1-\left(1-\Theta\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)\right)\,dx=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
.
Let
U\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
V\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
be scaled and duplicated vector representations of
u
and
v
, respectively.

(1)

Use a set of exponential random variables to form a vector
V
of duplicated and scaled coordinates of
v
.

(2)

Hash the coordinates of
V
into a
CountSketch
data structure with
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
buckets.

(3)

Use the same exponential random variables to perform perfect
L_{2}
sampling on
u
to obtain a coordinate
(i,j)
and an unbiased estimate
\widehat{u_{i,j}}
to
u_{i,j}
.

(4)

Let
\widehat{U}
be an unbiased estimate of
\|u\|_{2}^{2}
with second moment
\mathcal{O}\left(\|u\|_{2}^{4}\right)
.

(5)

Query
CountSketch
for an unbiased estimate
\widehat{v_{i,j}}
to
v_{i,j}
and set an estimator as
\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{i,j}}\left(\widehat{u_{i,j}}\right)^{p-3}
.

(6)

Output the mean of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot n^{1-2/p}\right)
such estimators.

Fig. 9
:
F_{p}
difference estimator for
\langle v,u^{p-1}\rangle
with integer
p>2
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists an algorithm that uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
and outputs an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\langle v,u^{p-1}\rangle
.

Proof.

For an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
, let
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and
u
be the frequency vector induced by updates from time
t_{2}
to
t
exclusive, with
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
We use
Sampler
to sample indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n]
with so that each sample is a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{u_{a}^{2}}{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.
For each
b\in[k]
, we also obtain an unbiased estimate
\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}
of
v_{j_{b}}
from the
CountSketch
data structure.
We also obtain an unbiased estimate
\widehat{U}
of
\|u\|_{2}^{2}
.
Observe that

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}\cdot\widehat{u_{j_{b}}^{p-3}}\right]=\|u\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{a\in[n]}\left(\frac{u_{a}^{2}}{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)v_{a}\cdot u_{a}^{p-3}=\langle v,u^{p-1}\rangle.

To analyze the variance of
\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}\left(\widehat{u_{j_{b}}}\right)^{p-3}
, we first observe that by the min-stability of exponential random variables, the maximum coordinate of the scaled vector
V
is distributed as
\|V\|_{2}/E^{1/2}
for an exponential random variable
E
.
If the coordinate
a\in[n]
from the unscaled vector
v
is sampled, this maximum coordinate is also
v_{a}/e_{a,j}^{1/2}
for an exponential random variable
e_{a,j}
.
We have that
v_{a}/e_{a,j}^{1/2}\geq\|V_{-a,j}\|_{2}/E^{1/2}
, where
V_{-a}
indicates the vector
V
with the
a
-th entry set to zero.
Since
E=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
with probability
1-\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
, then we have
e_{a,j}\leq\frac{\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)\cdot v_{a}^{2}}{\|V\|_{2}^{2}}
.
Hence
e_{a,j}
has expectation
\frac{\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)\cdot v_{a}^{2}}{\|V\|_{2}^{2}}
so that by using
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
buckets,
CountSketch
outputs an estimate
\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}
with variance
v_{a}^{2}
if
a\in[n]
is sampled in iteration
b
.
Thus, there exists a constant
C>0
such that the variance of
\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}\left(\widehat{u_{j_{b}}}\right)^{p-3}
is at most

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}\cdot\widehat{u_{j_{b}}^{p-3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[n]}\left(\frac{u_{a}^{2}}{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot C\|u\|_{2}^{4}\cdot v_{a}^{2}\cdot u_{a}^{2p-6}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[n]}2C\cdot u_{a}^{2p-4}v_{a}^{2}\cdot\|u\|_{2}^{2}.

By Hölder’s inequality, the variance is at most

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{U}\cdot\widehat{v_{j_{b}}}\cdot\widehat{u_{j_{b}}^{p-3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\cdot\|u\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left(\sum_{a\in[n]}v_{a}^{p}\right)^{2/p}\left(\sum_{a\in[n]}u_{a}^{2p}\right)^{1-2/p}

\displaystyle=2C\cdot\|u\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|u\|_{2p}^{2p-4}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\cdot n^{1-2/p}\cdot\|u\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|v\|_{p}^{2}\cdot\|u\|_{2p}^{2p-4}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\cdot n^{1-2/p}\cdot\|u\|_{p}^{2p-2}\|v\|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\cdot\gamma n^{1-2/p}\cdot n^{1-2/p}\|v\|_{p}^{2},

where the last inequality results from the fact that
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
and
p>2
.
Thus by setting
k=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
, we obtain an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\langle v,u^{p-1}\rangle
with constant probability.
We can then boost this probability to
1-\frac{\delta}{\poly(n)}
by repeating
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
times.
∎

Putting it all together.

We now give our
F_{p}
difference estimator for integer
p>2
using the above subroutines to approximate
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}\langle v^{i},u^{p-i}\rangle
.

Lemma 5.8

(
F_{p}
difference estimator for integer
p>2
)
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator for
F_{p}
that uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
.

Proof.

For an oblivious stream
{\mathcal{S}}
, let
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of
{\mathcal{S}}
from time
t_{1}
to
t_{2}
and
u
be the frequency vector induced by updates from time
t_{2}
to
t
exclusive, with
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
Observe that for integer
p
, we can expand

F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}\langle v^{i},u^{p-i}\rangle,

where we write
v^{p}
as the coordinate-wise
p
-th power of
v
.
Let
{\mathcal{H}}
be the set of coordinates output by
HeavyHitters
.
Moreover,

F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}+\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}.

By
Theorem
3.10
, there exists an algorithm
HeavyHitters
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2-2/p}\log^{2}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
space and outputs a list
{\mathcal{H}}
of coordinates and estimates
\widehat{v_{a}}
with additive error
\frac{\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}}{64\gamma}\|v\|_{p}
.
Thus using the estimates
\widehat{v_{a}}
along with the corresponding coordinates
u_{a}
gives an additive
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}
using
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma\log^{2}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,n^{1-2/p}\right)
space, for
p>2
.
By
Lemma
5.7
and
Lemma
5.6
, we similarly obtain an additive
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\binom{p}{i}v_{a}^{i}u_{a}^{p-i}
using
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)
space, where
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{p}
.
Thus by rescaling
\varepsilon
, we obtain an additive
\varepsilon\,F_{p}(v)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+u)-F_{p}(v)
using
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
bits of space.
∎

Using our difference estimator, we obtain a robust algorithm for
F_{p}
moment estimation for integer
p>2
.

Theorem 5.9

(Adversarially robust
F_{p}
streaming algorithm for integer
p>2
)
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and integer
p>2
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation for
F_{p}
that succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
and uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{5}n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
bits of space.

Proof.

For
p>2
,
F_{p}
is a monotonic function with
(\varepsilon,m)
-twist number
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\right)
, by
Observation
1.12
.
By
Lemma
5.8
and
Theorem
5.2
, there exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator that uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)

and a strong tracker for
F_{p}
that each uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{2}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right).

We use these subroutines in the framework of
Algorithm
1
.
Therefore,
Theorem
2.5
with
S_{1}(n,\delta,\varepsilon)=n^{1-2/p}\log^{3}n\log\frac{n}{\delta}
,
C=1
, and
S_{2}=0
proves that there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
for
F_{p}
that succeeds with constant probability and uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\log^{5}n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.
∎

6
Robust
F_{0}
Estimation

In this section, we use the framework of
Section
2
to give an adversarially robust streaming algorithm for the distinct elements problem or equivalently, the
F_{0}
moment estimation.
We again require an
F_{0}
strong tracker and an
F_{0}
difference estimator so that we can apply
Theorem
2.5
.
Fortunately, we can again use similar sketches for both the
F_{0}
strong stracker and the
F_{0}
difference estimator, similar to the approach for
F_{2}
moment estimation.
We can then optimize our algorithm beyond the guarantees of
Theorem
2.5
so that our final space guarantees in
Theorem
6.4
match the bounds in
Theorem
3.8
.
Note that this does not quite match the best known
F_{0}
strong-tracking algorithm on insertion-only streams
[
Bla20
]
, which uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\log n\right)
bits of space:

Theorem 6.1

(Oblivious
F_{0}
strong tracking)
.

[
Bla20
]

There exists an insertion-only streaming algorithm
{\textsc{F0Estimate}}(1,t,\varepsilon,\delta)
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log n\right)
bits of space and provides
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-strong
F_{0}
tracking.

Theorem
6.1
and other
F_{0}
approximation algorithms use a balls-and-bins argument with increasing levels of sophistication
[
BJK+02b
,
KNW10b
,
Bla20
]
.
We use a similar balls-and-bins argument, where each item is subsampled at a level
k
with probability
\frac{1}{2^{k}}
, to obtain an
F_{0}
difference estimator.
By counting the number of items in a level with
\Theta\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
items that survive the subsampling process for the stream
u
, it follows that the expected number of the distinct items in
v
but not
u
is
\Theta\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
.
Thus we obtain a
\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\right)
-approximation to
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
from this procedure by first running the balls-and-bins experiment on
u
and counting the number of bins that are occupied at some level
k
with
\Theta\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
survivors.
We then run the same balls-and-bins experiment on
v-u
by only counting the additional bins that are occupied at level
k
, and rescaling this number by
2^{k}
.
Note that additional bins only correspond to items in
v
but not
u
, which is exactly
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
.
Although level
k
does not necessarily give a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
, it does give a
\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\right)
-approximation to
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
, which translates to an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{0}(u)
approximation to
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
and is exactly the requirement for the
F_{0}
difference estimator, since
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)\leq\gamma F(u)
.

Lemma 6.2

(
F_{0}
difference estimator)
.

There exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-
difference estimator
for
F_{0}
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\log n\right)
bits of space.

Proof.

Suppose we would like to estimate
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)=\gamma\cdot F_{0}(u)
for
\gamma=\Omega(\varepsilon)
and we subsample with probability
\frac{1}{2^{k}}
so that
\frac{F_{0}(u)}{2^{k}}=\Theta\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
.
Then in expectation, the number of sampled items in
v
that are not in
u
is
\frac{F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)}{2^{k}}=\Theta\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
.
Hence if
X
is the number of survivors from
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
at level
k
, then
\mathbb{E}\left[2^{k}\cdot X\right]=F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
and the variance of
2^{k}\cdot X
is at most

2^{k}\cdot(F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u))\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\cdot F_{0}(u)}{\gamma}\cdot(F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u))\leq\varepsilon^{2}(F_{0}(u))^{2}.

Thus, the
2^{k}\cdot X
gives an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{0}
approximation to
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
with constant probability.
The probability can then be boosted to at least
1-\delta
by taking the median of
\log\frac{1}{\delta}
parallel instances.
Since the algorithm maintains
\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}
sampled items, the total space naïvely required is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma\log n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
.
By hashing to
P=\poly\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon},\log n,\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
buckets, the space bound can be further improved to
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\log n\right)
, e.g., by first composing a hash function
h_{1}:[n]\to[P^{3}]
and then a hash function
h_{2}:[P^{3}]\to[P]
, along the lines of
[
KNW10b
,
Bla20
]
.
This suffices for a difference estimator at a single point in time; to obtain the strong tracking property, we note that both the estimator and
F_{0}(v)-F_{0}(u)
are monotonic.
Hence, it suffices to take a union bound over
\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
times when the difference increases by a factor of
(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))
.
Therefore, the total space required is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\log n\right)
.
∎

Note that the difference estimator in
Lemma
6.2
only requires pairwise independence and thus can be derandomized using a hash function that can be stored using
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
bits of space.
We now use our difference estimator to get an adversarially robust streaming algorithm for the distinct elements problem.

Theorem 6.3
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{0}
that succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
and uses space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n\right).

Proof.

First note that
F_{0}
is a monotonic function with
(\varepsilon,m)
-twist number
\lambda=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\right)
for
m=\poly(n)
, by
Observation
1.12
.
By
Theorem
6.1
and
Lemma
6.2
, there exist a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-difference estimator that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\log n\right)
bits of space and an oblivious strong tracker for
F_{0}
that uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log n\right)
bits of space, which can be used in the framework of
Algorithm
1
.
We thus apply
Theorem
2.5
with
S_{1}(n,\delta,\varepsilon)=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}
,
C=1
, and
S_{2}(n,\delta,\varepsilon)=\log n
.
Thus the total space is

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n\right).

∎

Optimized
F_{0}
Algorithm.

To improve the space requirements, we can again observe that it suffices to maintain sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
for
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
values of
a
at a time, instead of maintaining all sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
simultaneously.
By the same argument as before, it suffices to maintain only the most sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,c}
for only the smallest
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
values of
i
that are at least
a
since the output increases by a factor of
2
each time
a
increases and thus any larger index will have only missed
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)
fraction of the
F_{0}
of the stream.
Hence, any larger index still outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation once it becomes initialized.

Theorem 6.4
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an adversarially robust streaming algorithm that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation for
F_{0}
that succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
and uses total space

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{4}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right).

Proof.

At any point, there are
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active indices
a
and
\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)
active indices
c
corresponding to sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
.
Because there are
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
total indices
a
over the stream, we set
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\poly\left(\log n,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)}
.
Thus from
Theorem
2.5
and
Theorem
6.3
that for fixed
a
, the total space that sketches
{\mathcal{A}}_{a}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{a,c}
use across the
\beta
granularities is

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)+\log n\right),

since we have
S_{1}(n,\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)
,
C=1
, and
S_{2}(n,\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\log n
.
However, there are only
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active indices
a
simultaneously maintained by the optimized algorithm.
For each active index
a
, the sketches use
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)+\log n\right)
bits of space.
As there are
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
active indices of
a
, consisting of
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
subroutines, then it takes
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log n\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
additional bits of space to store the splitting times for each of the
