We show
Algorithm
9
gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.
For
p\in(0,1]
, the algorithm is nearly identical to that of
p\in[1,2]
.
However due to sub-additivity, the
MergeSW
subroutine now merges two sketches when the sum of their contributions from their difference estimator is small, rather than the sum of their moments is small.
We also show
Algorithm
9
with the modified
MergeSW
subroutine gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
for
p\in(0,1]
.

Lemma 8.11

(Correctness of sliding window algorithm)
.

For
p\in(0,2]
,
Algorithm
9
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.

Theorem 8.12
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in(0,2]
, there exists a one-pass algorithm in the sliding window model that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to the
L_{p}
norm with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.
The algorithm uses
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
bits of space for
p=2
.
For
p\in(0,2)
, the algorithm uses space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n(\log\log n)^{2}\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.

Proof.

Consider
Algorithm
5
and note that it suffices to output a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to the
F_{p}
-moment of the window.
Thus correctness follows from
Lemma
8.11
.

To analyze the space complexity for
p=2
, we can use the strong tracker of
Theorem
3.1
and the difference estimator of
Lemma
3.6
.
Observe that running a single instance of these subroutines with accuracy parameter
\eta
and failure probability
\delta
requires space
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log n}{\eta^{2}}\left(\log\log n+\log\frac{1}{\eta}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)
.
We set the probability of failure to be
\delta=\frac{1}{\poly(n,m)}
so that by a union bound, all algorithms in the stream are simultaneously correct at all points in time, with probability
1-\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.

By
Lemma
A.6
, we run
\mathcal{O}\left(2^{j}\right)
instances of the suffix difference estimator at level
j
for each
i
, with accuracy
\eta=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\right)
, ratio parameter
\gamma=2^{3-j}
, and
\delta=\frac{1}{\poly(n,m)}
.
Hence, the total space used by level
j
for a fixed
i
is

\mathcal{O}\left(2^{j}\cdot\frac{\gamma\,\log^{2}n}{\eta^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n\log^{2}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right).

Summing across all
j\in[\beta]
for
\beta=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
levels, then the total space is for a fixed
i
is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{2}n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.
Since
i=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
, then the total space is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.

For
p<2
, we can use the strong tracker of
Theorem
4.2
and the difference estimator of
Lemma
4.12
.
Since a single instance of these subroutines with accuracy parameter
\eta=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\right)
and failure probability
\delta=\frac{1}{\poly(n,m)}
requires space at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log^{2}n}{\eta^{2}}(\log\log n)^{2}\right)
, then the total space is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{3}n(\log\log n)^{2}\log^{3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
.
∎

8.2
Entropy Estimation in the Sliding Window Model

In this section, we show how our adversarially robust
F_{p}
estimation algorithms gives a black-box algorithm for entropy estimation.
For a frequency vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we define the Shannon entropy by
H=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\log v_{i}
.

Observation 8.13
.

Any algorithm that gives an
\varepsilon
-additive approximation of the Shannon Entropy
H(v)
also gives a
(1+\varepsilon)
-multiplicative approximation of the function
h(v):=2^{H(v)}
(and vice versa).

Thus for the remainder of the section, we focus on obtaining a
(1+\varepsilon)
-multiplicative approximation of
h(v)=2^{H(v)}
.

Lemma 8.14

(Section 3.3 in
[
HNO08
]
)
.

For
k=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log m
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{12(k+1)^{3}\log m}
, there exists an efficiently computable set
\{y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}\}
with
y_{i}\in(0,2)
for all
i
and an efficiently computable deterministic function that takes
(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})
-approximations to
F_{y_{i}}(v)
and outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
h(v)=2^{H(v)}
.

The set
\{y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}\}
in
Lemma
8.14
can computed as follows, described by
[
HNO08
]
.
Let
\ell=\frac{1}{2(k+1)\log m}
and
f(z)=\frac{(k^{2}\ell)z-\ell(k^{2}+1)}{2k^{2}+1}
.
Then for each
y_{i}
, we have
y_{i}=1+f(\cos(i\pi/k))
, so that the set
\{y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}\}
in
Lemma
8.14
can be computed in linear time.
A
(1+\varepsilon)
-multiplicative approximation to
h(v)=2^{H(v)}
can then be computed from
2^{P(0)}
, where
P(x)
is the degree
k
polynomial interpolated at the points
y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}
, so that
P(y_{i})=F_{y_{i}}(v)
for each
i
.

Theorem 8.15
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a sliding window algorithm that outputs an additive
\varepsilon
-approximation to Shannon entropy and uses
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{5}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
bits of space and succeeds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

Proof.

By
Observation
8.13
and
Lemma
8.14
, it suffices to obtain adversarially robust
(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})
-approximation algorithms to
F_{y_{i}}(v)
for all
y_{i}\in(0,2)
in the set
\{y_{0},\ldots,y_{k}\}
, where
k=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log m
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{12(k+1)^{3}\log m}
.
By
Theorem
8.12
with accuracy parameter
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we can obtain such robust algorithms approximating each
F_{y_{i}}(v)
, using space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}\log^{2}n\right)
.
Moreover, with a rescaling of the failure probability
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\poly(n,m)}
in both
Lemma
4.12
and
Theorem
4.2
, then all algorithms simultaneously succeed with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Hence for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{12(k+1)^{3}\log m}
and
\log m=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
, each of the
\mathcal{O}\left(k\right)
algorithms use space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log^{5}n\right)
.
Since
k=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\log\log m
, the overall space complexity follows.
∎

8.3
Moment Estimation for Integer
p>2

In this section, we describe our algorithm to estimate the
F_{p}
moment in the sliding window model, for integer
p>2
.
Suppose that we have vectors
u
and
v
such that the vector
u
arrives before the vector
v
and
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)\leq 2^{p}F_{p}(u)
, for some
\gamma\leq 1
.
A crucial subroutine in
Algorithm
5
is the
MergeSW
subroutine, which controls the number of blocks in which the substream is partitioned into, at each granularity, and thus gives efficient bounds on the space of the algorithm.

Algorithm
11
merges separate blocks based on their contribution to the value of the difference
F_{p}(u+v)-F_{p}(v)
.
Rather than using such a merge subroutine in this section, we will again create
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
parallel instances as in
Algorithm
7
, corresponding to exponentially increasing guesses
2^{i}
for the value of
F_{p}(v)
and incur the additional
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
overhead in space.
We will then partition blocks based on their
F_{p}
values in comparison to the guess of
F_{p}(v)
.
Now if each block
u
is required to satisfy
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)\leq 2^{p}F_{p}(u)
for some
\gamma\approx\frac{1}{2^{j}}
, then we can assume our guess for
F_{p}(v)
is incorrect if the partitioning creates more than
2^{j}
blocks.
Hence, we can again assume that each block
u
satisfies
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)\leq 2^{p}F_{p}(u)
, at the cost of an additional
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
overhead in space.

As in
Section
8.1
, it remains to find a difference estimator for
F_{p}(u+v)-F_{p}(v)
, i.e., an algorithm with space dependency
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,n^{1-2/p}\right)
.
Observe that
F_{p}(u+v)-F_{p}(v)=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\binom{p}{k}\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
.
We estimate each term
\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
separately.

We first use a heavy-hitter algorithm
HHEst
to simultaneously find all heavy-hitters
a\in[n]
such that
u_{a}\geq\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}\,L_{p}(v)
across all vectors
u
induced by the blocks.
These coordinates form a set
{\mathcal{H}}
and we read off the corresponding coordinates of
v
to estimate
\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
for each
k
.
For
a\notin{\mathcal{H}}
, we analyze separate algorithms for estimating
\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\langle u,v^{p-1}\rangle
and
\sum_{a\notin{\mathcal{H}}}\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
for
k\geq 2
, though the analysis is similar for both algorithms.
Thus we require the following modification to subroutine
StitchSW
.

Algorithm 13

Subroutine
StitchSW
of
Algorithm
5
: output estimate of
F_{p}
on the sliding window

1:

Let
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.

2:

c_{0}\leftarrow t_{i}

3:

X\leftarrow{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i},m,\eta,\delta)

4:

for

j\in[\beta]

do

\triangleright
Stitch sketches

5:

Let
a
be the smallest index such that
t_{i,j,a}\geq c_{j-1}

6:

Let
b
be the largest index such that
t_{i,j,b}\leq m-W+1

7:

c_{j}\leftarrow t_{i,j,b}

8:

Y_{j}\leftarrow\sum_{k=a}^{b-1}{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},m,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)

9:

for
each heavy-hitter
k
output by
{\mathcal{H}}:={\textsc{HHEst}}(t_{i},m,\eta,\delta)

do

10:

Let
g_{k}
be the estimated frequency of
k
in times
[c_{\beta},m]
.

11:

Let
h_{k}
be the estimated frequency of
k
in times
[m-W+1,m]
.

12:

W_{k}\leftarrow(g_{k})^{p}-(h_{k})^{p}

13:

return

Z:=X-\sum_{j=1}^{\beta}Y_{j}-\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{H}}}W_{k}

Our analysis uses a level set argument.
For a sufficiently large constant
\alpha>0
, let
i,j\in[\alpha\log n]
.
Then we define the contribution
C_{i,j,k}
of level set
L_{i,j}
for
\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
by

\sum_{a\in n}\left[u_{a}^{k}v_{a}^{p-k}{\mathbf{1}}\left(u_{a}\in\left[\frac{\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)}{2^{i}},\frac{\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)}{2^{i-1}}\right]\right){\mathbf{1}}\left(v_{a}\in\left[\frac{L_{p}(v)}{2^{j}},\frac{L_{p}(v)}{2^{j-1}}\right]\right)\right].

Then we say contribution
C_{i,j,k}
of level set
L_{i,j}
is significant if
C_{i,j,k}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}\,F_{p}(v)
.

Lemma 8.16
.

If
C_{i,j,k}
is significant, then
2^{|i-j|}\leq\frac{\gamma^{k/(p(p-k))}p^{(p+1)/(p-k)}\log^{2/(p-k)}n}{\varepsilon^{1/(p-k)}}
.

Proof.

Suppose without loss of generality that
i\leq j
, so that
j=i+\ell
for some
\ell\geq 0
.
Because
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
, then there can be at most
2^{ip}
indices
a\in[n]
such that
u_{a}\in\left[\frac{\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)}{2^{i}},\frac{\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)}{2^{i-1}}\right]
.
Hence, we have

C_{i,j,k}\leq 2^{ip}\cdot\left(\frac{\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)}{2^{i}}\right)^{k}\cdot\left(\frac{L_{p}(v)}{2^{j}}\right)^{p-k}=\frac{\gamma^{k/p}}{2^{\ell(p-k)}}\,F_{p}(v).

Since
C_{i,j,k}
is significant, then
C_{i,j,k}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}\,F_{p}(v)
, which implies that
2^{\ell(p-k)}\leq\frac{\gamma^{k/p}p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}{\varepsilon}
and thus
2^{j}\leq 2^{i}\,\frac{\gamma^{k/(p(p-k))}p^{(p+1)/(p-k)}\log^{2/(p-k)}n}{\varepsilon^{1/(p-k)}}
.
∎

We first run a heavy-hitters algorithm to find all indices
a\in[n]
such that

u_{a}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{100p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}\gamma^{1/p}L_{p}(v)\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{100p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}L_{p}(u),

which takes
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,n^{1-2/p}\right)
space, since
F_{p}(u)\leq\gamma F_{p}(v)
.
Thus we bound the variance for a significant level set
L_{i,j}
with
2^{i}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{100p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}
.

(1)

Find a list
{\mathcal{H}}
that includes all
a\in[n]
with
u_{a}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{100p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}\|u\|_{p}
.

(2)

Using
CountSketch
, obtain an estimate
\widehat{u_{a}}
to
u_{a}
with additive error
\frac{\varepsilon}{100p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}\|u\|_{p}
for each
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
and let
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the vector such that
h_{a}=\widehat{u_{a}}
if
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
and zero otherwise.

(3)

Perform perfect
L_{2}
sampling on
w:=u-h
to obtain a set
{\mathcal{S}}
of size
k=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
.

(4)

Obtain an estimate
\widehat{w_{a}}
to
w_{a}
for each
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

(5)

Let
\widehat{W}
be a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
\|w\|_{2}^{2}
.

(6)

Output
W+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\binom{p}{k}\left(\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\widehat{v_{a}}^{k},u_{a}^{p-k}+W\cdot\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{S}}}\widehat{w_{a}}^{k-2},u_{a}^{p-k}\right)
.

Lemma 8.17
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists an algorithm that uses space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
and outputs an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(u)
approximation to
\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
with high probability for any
k\geq 2
.

Proof.

We first show that our algorithm gives an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}}F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
with
k\geq 2
.
To that end, we show that our algorithm gives an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}\log^{2}n}F_{p}(v)
approximation to each contribution
C_{i,j,k}
of level set
L_{i,j}
.
Let
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the vector such that
h_{a}=\widehat{u_{a}}
if
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
and zero otherwise and let
w:=u-h
.

We use
Sampler
to sample indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}\in[n]
with so that each sample is a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.
We obtain unbiased estimates to
\widehat{w_{j_{1}}^{k-2}},\ldots,\widehat{w_{j_{\ell}}^{k-2}}
of
w_{j_{1}}^{k-2},\ldots,w_{j_{\ell}}^{k-2}
through
Lemma
5.5
.
We also obtain a
(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))
-approximation unbiased estimate
W
of
\|w\|_{2}^{2}
.
Thus for each
b\in[\ell]
, the product
\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{k-2}}\cdot W\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-k}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{k-2}}\cdot W\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\langle\widehat{u_{a}}^{k},v_{a}^{p-k}\rangle+\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\cap{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{k-2}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{p-k}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{k-2}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{p-k}.

Observe that since
\widehat{u_{a}}
is an additive
\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}\,L_{p}(v)
-approximation to
u_{a}
for
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
, then
|w_{a}|\leq\varepsilon|u_{a}|
, so that the second summation is at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\,\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
.
Moreover, we have
w_{a}=u_{a}
for
a\in[n]\setminus{\mathcal{H}}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{k-2}}\cdot W\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-k}\right]

\displaystyle\in(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))|\langle w^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle|+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}.

For
a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}
, we have
w_{a}\leq u_{a}\leq\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}\,L_{p}(v)
.
Thus, the variance is at most

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{k-2}}\cdot W\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{w_{a}^{2}}{\|w\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{2k-4}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{4}\cdot v_{a}^{2p-2k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))w_{a}^{2k-2}\cdot\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{2p-2k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\frac{\varepsilon^{p}\gamma^{2}\,(L_{p}(v))^{2p-2}}{2^{2ip}}\frac{\gamma^{2k/p}p^{p(2p+2)}\log^{4}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\|w\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{2^{2ip}\varepsilon^{2}},

for some constant
C>0
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{w_{j_{b}}^{k-2}}\cdot W\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}2^{ip}\cdot\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{2^{2ip}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}n\right)\cdot\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Thus by setting
\ell=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
, we obtain an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\langle u^{k},v^{p-k}\rangle
for
k\geq 2
, with constant probability.
We can then boost this probability to
1-\frac{\delta}{\poly(n)}
by repeating
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
times.
∎

We use a similar approach to estimate
\langle u,v^{p-1}\rangle
.

(1)

Use a set of exponential random variables to form a vector
W
of duplicated and scaled coordinates of
w
.

(2)

Hash the coordinates of
W
into a
CountSketch
data structure with
\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
buckets.

(3)

Use the same exponential random variables to perform perfect
L_{2}
sampling on
v
to obtain a coordinate
(i,j)
and an unbiased estimate
\widehat{v_{i,j}}
to
v_{i,j}
.

(4)

Let
\widehat{V}
be an unbiased estimate of
\|v\|_{2}^{2}
with second moment
\mathcal{O}\left(\|v\|_{2}^{4}\right)
.

(5)

Query
CountSketch
for an unbiased estimate
\widehat{w_{i,j}}
to
w_{i,j}
and set an estimator as
\widehat{V}\cdot\widehat{w_{i,j}}\left(\widehat{v_{i,j}}\right)^{p-3}
.

(6)

Output the mean of
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot n^{1-2/p}\right)
such estimators.

Lemma 8.18
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists an algorithm that uses space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
and outputs an additive
\varepsilon\cdot F_{p}(u)
approximation to
\langle u,v^{p-1}\rangle
with high probability.

Proof.

We use
Sampler
to sample indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}\in[n]
with so that each sample is a coordinate
a\in[n]
with probability
\frac{v_{a}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}
.
We obtain unbiased estimates to
\widehat{v_{j_{1}}^{p-1}},\ldots,\widehat{v_{j_{\ell}}^{p-1}}
of
v_{j_{1}}^{p-1},\ldots,w_{j_{\ell}}^{p-1}
through
Lemma
5.5
.
We also obtain a
(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))
-approximation unbiased estimate
V
of
\|v\|_{2}^{2}
.
Thus for each
b\in[\ell]
, the product
\widehat{w_{j_{b}}}\cdot V\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-3}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}}\cdot V\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-3}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{H}}}\langle\widehat{u_{a}},v_{a}^{p-1}\rangle+\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\cap{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{v_{a}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|v\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{p-3}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{v_{a}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|w\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{p-3}.

Observe that since
\widehat{u_{a}}
is a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
u_{a}
for
a\in{\mathcal{H}}
, then
|w_{a}|\leq\varepsilon|u_{a}|
, so that the second summation is at most
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right)\,\langle u,v^{p-1}\rangle
.
Moreover, we have
w_{a}=u_{a}
for
a\in[n]\setminus{\mathcal{H}}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w_{j_{b}}}\cdot V\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-3}\right]

\displaystyle\in(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))|\langle w,v^{p-1}\rangle|+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}.

For
a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}
, we have
w_{a}\leq u_{a}\leq\varepsilon\gamma^{1/p}\,L_{p}(v)
.
Thus, the variance is at most

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{w_{j_{b}}}\cdot V\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\left(\frac{v_{a}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\right)\cdot w_{a}^{2}\cdot(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\|v\|_{2}^{4}\cdot v_{a}^{2p-6}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))w_{a}^{2}\cdot\|v\|_{2}^{2}\cdot v_{a}^{2p-6}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}(1\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\right))\frac{\varepsilon^{p}\gamma^{2/p}\,(L_{p}(v))^{2p-2}}{2^{2ip}}\frac{\gamma^{2k/p}p^{p(2p+2)}\log^{4}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\|v\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\sum_{a\in L_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}}\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma^{2/p}\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{2^{2ip}\varepsilon^{2}},

for some constant
C>0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\Var\left(\widehat{w_{j_{b}}}\cdot V\cdot v_{j_{b}}^{p-3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}2^{ip}\cdot\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma^{2/p}\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{2^{2ip}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}n\right)\cdot\frac{Cn^{1-2/p}\varepsilon^{p}\gamma\,(F_{p}(v))^{2}\log^{4}n}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Hence for
\gamma\geq\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{p}\right)
, we have that by setting
\ell=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}n^{1-2/p}\right)
, we obtain an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{p+1}}\cdot F_{p}(v)
approximation to
\langle u,v^{p-1}\rangle
, with constant probability.
We can then boost this probability to
1-\frac{\delta}{\poly(n)}
by repeating
\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)
times.
∎

Lemma 8.19

(
F_{p}
difference estimator)
.

For integer
p>2
, there exists a
(\gamma,\varepsilon,\delta)
-
difference estimator
for
F_{p}
that uses space
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\gamma n^{1-2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
.

By applying the same argument as
Lemma
8.8
using the difference estimators in this section, we have:

Lemma 8.20

(Correctness of sliding window algorithm)
.

For integer
p>2
,
Algorithm
5
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.

Theorem 8.21
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
and integer
p>2
, there exists a one-pass algorithm in the sliding window model that outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to the
L_{p}
norm with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
and uses
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,n^{1-2/p}\right)
bits of space.

Proof.

Consider
Algorithm
5
and note that it suffices to output a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to the
F_{p}
-moment of the window.
Thus correctness follows from
Lemma
8.20
.

We use the strong tracker of
Theorem
5.2
and the difference estimator of
Lemma
8.19
.
By
Lemma
A.6
, we run
\mathcal{O}\left(2^{j}\right)
instances of the suffix difference estimator at level
j
for each
i
, with accuracy
\eta=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\right)
, ratio parameter
\gamma=2^{3-j}
, and
\delta=\frac{1}{\poly(n,m)}
.
Hence, the total space used by level
j
for a fixed
i
is

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(2^{j}\cdot\frac{\gamma\,n^{1-2/p}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{n^{1-2/p}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right).

Summing across all
j\in[\beta]
for
\beta=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
levels and all values of
i=\mathcal{O}\left(\log n\right)
, then the algorithm uses
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,n^{1-2/p}\right)
bits of space in total.
∎

Appendix A
Appendix: Moment Estimation on Sliding Windows

In this section, we give the full details of the proofs from
Section
8
.

A.1
Moment Estimation for
p\in[1,2]

Lemma A.1

(Constant factor partitions in top level)
.

Let
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
,
F_{p}(W)\leq F_{p}(t_{i},m)\leq 2F_{p}(W)
and
\frac{1}{2}F_{p}(W)\leq F_{p}(t_{i+1},m)\leq F_{p}(W)
.

Proof.

Observe that in the
MergeSW
subroutine, we only delete a timestamp
t_{i^{\prime}}
if
{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i^{\prime}+2},t,\eta,\delta)\geq\frac{9}{10}{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i^{\prime}},t,\eta,\delta)
at some point
t
.
Since
\eta=\frac{\varepsilon}{1024\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\leq\frac{1}{1024}
, we have that conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
,
F_{p}(t_{i^{\prime}+2},t)\left(1+\frac{1}{1024}\right)\geq\frac{9}{10}\left(1-\frac{1}{1024}\right)F_{p}(t_{i^{\prime}},t)
.
Thus,
F_{p}(t_{i^{\prime}+2},t)\geq\frac{7}{8}F_{p}(t_{i^{\prime}},t)
.

We have either
t_{i}=m-W+1
and
t_{i+1}=m-W+2
in which case the statement is trivially true or timestamps
t_{i}
and
t_{i+1}
at some point
t\leq m
must have satisfied
F_{p}(t_{i+1},t)\geq\frac{7}{8}F_{p}(t_{i},t)
so that
t_{i}+1
was removed from the set of timestamps.
Thus,
F_{p}(t_{i+1},t)\geq\frac{7}{8}F_{p}(t_{i},t)
.
By
Lemma
8.10
for
p\leq 2
and the definition of smoothness,
F_{p}(t_{i+1},t)\geq\frac{7}{8}F_{p}(t_{i},t)
implies
F_{p}(t_{i+1},m)\geq 2F_{p}(t_{i},m)
for all
m\geq t
.
Since
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
, then
F_{p}(t_{i},m)\geq F_{p}(m-w+1,m)=F_{p}(W)\geq F_{p}(t_{i+1},m)
and the conclusion follows.
∎

We now show an upper bound on the moment of each substream whose contribution is estimated by the difference estimator, i.e., the difference estimators are well-defined.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
p\in[1,2]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and all
k
, either
t_{i,j,k+1}=t_{i,j,k}+1
or
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(W)
.

Proof.

Suppose
t_{i,j,k+1}\neq t_{i,j,k}+1
and
i+1
is the smallest index such that
t_{i+1}>m-W+1
.
Then at some time during the stream, the timestamp
t_{i,j,k}+1
must have been removed from the set of timestamps tracked by the algorithm.
Thus at some time
t
,
{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},1,\delta)\leq 2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta)
so that conditioned on the correctness of the algorithms
{\mathcal{A}}
providing
2
-approximations to the respectively quantities,
\frac{1}{2}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1})\leq 2^{-j-9}F_{p}(t_{i},t)
.
By
Lemma
A.1
, we have that
F_{p}(t_{i},t)\leq 2F_{p}(W)
.
Hence,
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(W)
.
∎

Lemma A.3

(Accuracy of difference estimators)
.

Let
p\in[1,2]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and all
k
,
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
gives an additive
\eta\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
approximation to
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Recall that for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator to be well-defined, we require
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t_{i,j,k})\leq\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:m)
.
By
Lemma
A.2
, we have that
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(W)
.
Since
F_{p}(W)\leq F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:m)
and
\gamma_{j}=2^{-j+3}
, then we certainly have
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t_{i,j,k})\leq\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:m)
.
Hence by the definition of a (suffix-pivoted) difference estimator, we have that
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
gives an additive
\eta\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
approximation to
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
with probability at least
1-\delta
.
∎

Lemma A.4

(Geometric upper bounds on splitting times)
.

Let
p\in[1,2]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and each
k
that either
t_{i,j,k+1}=t_{i,j,k}+1
or
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},m)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)\leq 2^{-j/p-2}p\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)
.

Proof.

Suppose
t_{i,j,r+1}\neq t_{i,j,r}+1
.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(W)
by
Lemma
A.2
.
Moreover,
F_{p}(W)\leq F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)
.
By the smoothness of
F_{p}
in
Lemma
8.10
,
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},m)\leq(1+2^{-(j-7)/p}p)\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)
.
Hence for
p\in[1,2]
, we have
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},m)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)\leq 2^{-j/p-2}p\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},m)
.
∎

Lemma A.5

(Geometric lower bounds on splitting times)
.

Let
p\in[1,2]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and each
k
that

F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k+1})>2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(W).

Proof.

Note that conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k+1})\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k+1},1,\delta)
and
2\cdot{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i},m,\eta,\delta)\geq F_{p}(t_{i},m)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot F_{p}
.
Since
MergeSW
did not merge the timestamp
t_{i,j,k}
then it follows that
{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k+1},1,\delta)>2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta)
.
Hence, it follows that
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k+1})>2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(W)
.
∎

Next we bound the number of instances
k
of the level
j
difference estimators.

Lemma A.6

(Number of level
j
difference estimators)
.

Let
p\in[1,2]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
, that
k\leq 2^{j+10}
for any
t_{i,j,k}
.

Proof.

For a fixed
j
, let
r
be the number of instances of
t_{i,j,k}
.
For each
k\in[r]
, let
u_{k}
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,k-1}
and
t_{i,j,k}
.
Let
u
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i+1}
and
t
and let
v
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i}
and
t
.
Then by
Lemma
A.5
, we have
F_{p}(u_{k}+u_{k+1})>2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(W)
.
By
Lemma
A.1
,
F_{p}(t_{i},m)\leq 2F_{p}(W)
.
For
p\in[1,2]
, we have
F_{p}(t_{i},m)\geq\sum_{\ell=1}^{r/2}F_{p}(u_{2\ell-1}+u_{\ell})
.
Thus for
r>2^{j+10}
implies that
F_{p}(t_{i},m)>2F_{p}(W)
, which is a contradiction.
Similarly for
p<1
, we have that

F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=k}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)-F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)\geq 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v).

Hence
r>2^{j+10}
implies

F_{p}(v)=F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)>F_{p}(v),

which is a contradiction.
Hence, it follows that
r\leq 2^{j+10}
.
∎

We next bound the number of level
j
difference estimators that can occur from the end of the previous level
j-1
difference estimator to the time when the sliding window begins.
We say a difference estimator
{\mathcal{C}}_{i,j,k}
is active if
k\in[a_{j},b_{j}]
for the indices
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
defined in
Algorithm
9
.
The active difference estimators in each level will be the algorithms whose output are subtracted from the initial rough estimate to form the final estimate of
F_{2}(W)
.

Lemma A.7

(Number of active level
j
difference estimators)
.

Let
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
in
Algorithm
9
succeed.
For
p\in[1,2]
and each
j\in[\beta]
, let
a_{j}
be the smallest index such that
t_{i,j,a_{j}}\geq c_{j-1}
and let
b_{j}
be the largest index such that
t_{i,j,b_{j}}\leq m-W+1
.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have
b_{j}-a_{j}\leq 512
.

Proof.

Conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and all
k
,

2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(W)<F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(W),

by
Lemma
A.4
and
Lemma
A.5
.
By
Lemma
A.1
,
F_{p}(t_{i},m)\leq 2F_{p}(W)
.
Thus for
j=1
, we have
j\leq 512
, so
b_{j}-a_{j}\leq 1024
.
For
j>1
, note that if
b_{j}-a_{j}>1024
, then we have
F_{p}(t_{i,j,a_{j}},t_{i,j,b_{j}})>2^{-j-6}\cdot F_{p}(W)
for
p\in[1,2]
.
Since
t_{i,j,a_{j}}=t_{i,j-1,b_{j-1}}=c_{j-1}
and
b_{j}\leq m-W+1
, then there exists an index
x>b_{j-1}
such that
t_{i,j-1,x}>m-W+1
, which contradicts the maximality of
c_{j-1}=t_{i,j-1,b_{j-1}}
.
∎

Lemma A.8

(Correctness of sliding window algorithm)
.

For
p\in[1,2]
,
Algorithm
9
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.

Proof.

Let
f
be the frequency vector induced by the window and let
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
and let
c_{0}=t_{i}
.
Let
u
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time
t_{i}
to
m
, so that we have
u\succeq f
coordinate-wise, since
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
For each
j\in[\beta]
, let
a_{j}
be the smallest index such that
t_{i,j,a_{j}}\geq c_{i-1}
and let
b_{j}
be the largest index such that
t_{i,j,b_{j}}\leq m-W+1
.
Since
MergeSW
does not merge indices of
t_{i,j,k}
that are indices of
t_{i,j-1,k^{\prime}}
, it follows that
t_{i,j,a_{j}}=c_{i-1}
.
Let
u_{j}
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time
a_{j}
to
b_{j}
.
Let
v
denote the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from
c_{\beta}
to
m-W+1
.
Thus, we have
u=\sum_{j=1}^{\beta}u_{j}+v+f
, so it remains to show that:

(1)

F_{p}(v+f)-F_{p}(f)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)

(2)

We have an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+f)
.

Combined, these two statements show that we have a multiplicative
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation of
F_{p}(f)
.

To show that
F_{p}(v+f)-F_{p}(f)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
, let
k
be the index such that
t_{i,\beta,k}=c_{\beta}
, so that
t_{i,\beta,k}\leq m-W+1\leq t_{i,\beta,k+1}
.
Note that if
t_{i,\beta,k}=m-W+1
then
v=0
and so the claim is trivially true.
Otherwise by the definition of the choice of
c_{\beta}
, we have that either
t_{i,\beta,k+1}>m-W+1
so that
t_{i,\beta,k+1}>t_{i,\beta,k}+1
.
By
Lemma
8.7
, it follows that
F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m)-F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)\leq 2^{-\beta/p-2}p\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)
.
Since
\beta={\left\lceil\log\frac{100p}{\varepsilon^{p}}\right\rceil}
, then it follows that
F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m)-F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)
.
By
Lemma
8.3
, we have that
\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)\leq 2F(W)=2F(f)
.
Thus,
F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m)-F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
.
Since the updates from the times between
t_{i,\beta,k}
and
m
form the vector
v+f
, we have

F_{p}(v+f)-F_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
.
Since
t_{i,\beta,k+1}>m-W+1
then by the monotonicity of
F_{p}
, we have that
F_{p}(v+f)-F_{p}(f)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
, as desired.

We next show that we have an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+f)
.
If we define

\Delta_{j}:=F_{p}\left(u-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}u_{k}\right)-F_{p}\left(u-\sum_{k=1}^{j}u_{k}\right),

then we have

\sum_{j=1}^{\beta}\Delta_{j}=F_{p}(u)-F_{p}(v+f).

Observe that
\Delta_{j}
is approximated by the active level
j
difference estimators.
By
Lemma
8.6
, there are at most
16
active indices at level
j
.
Since the difference estimators are well-defined by
Lemma
8.4
and each difference estimator
SDiffEst
uses accuracy parameter
\eta=\frac{\varepsilon}{1024\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}
, then the additive error in the estimation of
F_{p}(v+f)
incurred by
Y_{j}
is at most

16\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{1024\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\cdot F_{p}(u)=\frac{\varepsilon}{64\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\cdot F_{p}(u).

Summing across all
\beta\leq\log\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}
levels, then the total error in the estimation
Z
of
F_{p}(v+f)
across all levels
Y_{j}
with
j\in[\beta]
is at most

\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\cdot F_{p}(u)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{16}\cdot F_{p}(f).

Thus, we have an additive
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
approximation to
F_{p}(v+f)
as desired.
Since
F_{p}(v+f)-F_{p}(f)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot F_{p}(f)
, then
Algorithm
5
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.
∎

A.2
Moment Estimation for
p\in(0,1]

For
p\in(0,1]
, the algorithm is nearly identical to that of
p\in[1,2]
.
However, the
MergeSW
subroutine now merges two sketches when the sum of their contributions from their difference estimator is small, rather than the sum of their moments is small.
In particular, note the merge condition of
Algorithm
11
compared to
Algorithm
14
.

Algorithm 14

Subroutine
MergeSW
of
Algorithm
5
: removes extraneous subroutines

1:

Let
s
be the number of instances of
{\mathcal{A}}
and
p\in(0,1]
.

2:

\beta\leftarrow{\left\lceil\log\frac{100}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil}

3:

for

i\in[s]
,
j\in[\beta]

do

\triangleright
Difference estimator maintenance

4:

Let
r
be the number of times
t_{i,j,*}

5:

for

k\in[r-1]

do

\triangleright
Merges two algorithms with “small” contributions

6:

if

p<1
and
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)+{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)\leq 2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}_{i}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta)

then

7:

if

t_{i,j,k}\notin\{t_{i,j-1,*}\}

then

8:

Merge (add) the sketches for
{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},1,\delta)
and
{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},1,\delta)
.

9:

Merge (add) the sketches for
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
and
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
.

10:

Relabel the times
t_{i,j,*}
.

11:

for

i\in[s-2]

do

\triangleright
Smooth histogram maintenance

12:

if

{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i+2},t,\eta,\delta)\geq\frac{9}{10}{\mathcal{A}}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta)

then

13:

for

j\in[\beta]

do

14:

Append the times
t_{i+1,j,*}
to
\{t_{i,j,*}\}
.

15:

Delete
t_{i+1}
and all times
t_{i+1,*,*}
.

16:

Relabel the times
\{t_{i}\}
and
\{t_{i,j,*}\}
.

The analysis is mostly the same as the case for
p\in[1,2]
.
However, rather than bounding the moments of the frequency vectors induced by each block, we instead bound their contributions to the difference estimators.

Lemma A.9

(Accuracy of difference estimators)
.

Let
p\in(0,1]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
and
SDiffEst
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and all
k
,
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
gives an additive
\eta\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
approximation to
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
.

Proof.

Recall that for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator to be well-defined, we require
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t_{i,j,k})\leq\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
or
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t_{i,j,k})\leq\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
.
Observe that at the time
t
when the splitting time
t_{i,j,k}
is chosen and conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we must have

F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)=2^{-10}\cdot\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t).

By smoothness of
F_{p}
for
p\in(0,1]
in
Lemma
8.10
, it follows that

F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)\leq 2^{-10}\cdot\gamma_{j}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t).

Thus the conditions for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator hold, so
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},m,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
gives an additive
\eta\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:t)
approximation to
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}:m)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k}:m)
by the guarantees of the difference estimator.
∎

Lemma A.10

(Geometric upper bounds on splitting times)
.

Let
p\in(0,1]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
and
SDiffEst
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and each
k
that either
t_{i,j,k+1}=t_{i,j,k}+1
or
F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},t)\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+1},t)
.

Proof.

Suppose
t_{i,j,k+1}\neq t_{i,j,k}+1
.
Then at some time
x
, the timestamp
t_{i,j,k}+1
must have been merged by subroutine
MergeSW
with
p\in(0,1]
.
Thus

{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k^{\prime}},x,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)+{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k^{\prime}},t_{i,j,k+1},x,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)\leq 2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}_{i}(t_{i},x,\eta,\delta).

Let
u_{1}
be frequency vector representing the updates from time
t_{i,j,k}
to
t_{i,j,k^{\prime}}-1
,
u_{2}
represent times
t_{i,j,k^{\prime}}
to
t_{i,j,k+1}
, and
u
represent times
t_{i,j,k}
to
t_{i,j,k+1}
, so that
u_{1}+u_{2}=u
.
Let
x_{1}
represent the updates between time
t_{i,j,r+1}
and
x
and let
x_{2}
represent the updates between time
x+1
and
t
.
Since the difference estimators are well-defined by
Lemma
A.9
, we have that
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k^{\prime}},x,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
is at most a
2
-approximation to
F_{p}(u_{1}+u_{2}+x_{1})-F_{p}(u_{2}+x_{1})
and we similarly obtain a
2
-approximation to
F_{p}(u_{2}+x_{1})-F_{p}(x_{1})
.
Thus,

F_{p}(u+x_{1})-F_{p}(x_{1})=F_{p}(u_{1}+u_{2}+x_{1})-F_{p}(x_{1})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(x_{1}).

By the smoothness of
F_{p}
in
Lemma
8.10
for
p\in(0,1]
, we thus have that for any vector
x_{2}
,

F_{p}(u+v)-F_{p}(v)=F_{p}(u+x_{1}+x_{2})-F_{p}(x_{1}+x_{2})\leq 2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(x_{1}+x_{2})=2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(v).

∎

Lemma A.11

(Geometric lower bounds on splitting times)
.

Let
p\in(0,1]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
and
SDiffEst
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
Let
u
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,r}
and
t_{i,j,r+2}
and let
w
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,r+2}
and
t
.
Finally, let
w
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i}
and
t
.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that

F_{p}(u+v)-F_{p}(v)\geq 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{2}(w).

Proof.

Let
u_{1}
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,r}
and
t_{i,j,r+1}
and
u_{2}
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,r+1}
and
t_{i,j,r+2}
,
Recall that
MergeSW
for
p\in(0,1]
merges two instances of
SDiffEst
when

\displaystyle{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)+{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)\leq 2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}_{i}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta).

Thus we do not remove
t_{i,j,r+1}
from the list of timestamps, then we have

{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k-1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)+{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)>2^{-j-10}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}_{i}(t_{i},t,\eta,\delta).

Since the difference estimators are well-defined by
Lemma
A.9
, then
{\textsc{SDiffEst}}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k+1},t,\gamma_{j},\eta,\delta)
is at most a
2
-approximation to
F_{p}(u_{1}+u_{2}+v)-F_{p}(u_{2}+v)
and we similarly obtain a
2
-approximation to
F_{p}(u_{2}+v)-F_{p}(v)
.
Therefore,

F_{p}(u)-F_{p}(v)=F_{p}(u_{1}+u_{2}+v)-F_{p}(v)\geq 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(w).

∎

Next we bound the number of instances
k
of the level
j
difference estimators.

Lemma A.12

(Number of level
j
difference estimators)
.

Let
p\in(0,1]
and
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
and
SDiffEst
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
, that
k\leq 2^{j+10}
for any
t_{i,j,k}
.

Proof.

Let
r
be the number of instances of timestamps
t_{i,j,*}
for a fixed
j\in[\beta]
.
For each
k\in[r]
, let
u_{k}
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i,j,k-1}
and
t_{i,j,k}
.
Let
u
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i+1}
and
t
and let
v
denote the frequency vector between times
t_{i}
and
t
.
Then by
Lemma
A.11
, we have

F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=k}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)-F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)\geq 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v).

Thus if
r>2^{j+8}
, then we have

F_{p}(v)=F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}u_{\ell}\right)>F_{p}(v),

which is a contradiction.
Hence, it follows that
r\leq 2^{j+8}
.
∎

We next bound the number of level
j
difference estimators that can occur from the end of the previous level
j-1
difference estimator to the time when the sliding window begins.
We say a difference estimator
SDiffEst
is active if
k\in[a_{j},b_{j}]
for the indices
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
defined in
Algorithm
5
.
The active difference estimators in each level will be the algorithms whose output are subtracted from the initial rough estimate to form the final estimate of
F_{2}(W)
.

Lemma A.13

(Number of active level
j
difference estimators)
.

Let
i
be the largest index such that
t_{i}\leq m-W+1
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the event that all subroutines
{\mathcal{A}}
and
SDiffEst
in
Algorithm
5
succeed.
For
p\in(0,1]
and each
j\in[\beta]
, let
a_{j}
be the smallest index such that
t_{i,j,a_{j}}\geq c_{j-1}
and let
b_{j}
be the largest index such that
t_{i,j,b_{j}}\leq m-W+1
.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have
b_{j}-a_{j}\leq 512
.

Proof.

Conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}
, we have that for each
j\in[\beta]
and all
k
,

2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(W)<F_{p}(t_{i,j,k},t)-F_{p}(t_{i,j,k+2},t)\leq 2^{-j-6}\cdot F_{p}(W)t_{i,j,k+1},t),

by
Lemma
A.10
and
Lemma
A.11
.
By
Lemma
8.3
,
F_{p}(t_{i},m)\leq 2F_{p}(W)
.
Thus by a telescoping argument, we have that for
j=1
,
a_{j}-b_{j}<512
.

For
j>1
, suppose by way of contradiction that
b_{j}-a_{j}>512
.
Let
k=b_{j}-a_{j}
and for
x\in[k]
, let
u_{x}
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time
a_{j}+{x-1}
to
a_{j}+x
.
Let
u
be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time
t_{i,j,k}
to
m
and
v
be the frequency vector induced by the updates from
t_{i}
to
m
.
By
Lemma
A.11

F_{p}\left(u+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}u_{\ell}\right)-F_{p}(u)\geq 8\cdot 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v),

since there are at least
8
disjoint pairs of tuples
(i,j,\ell)
and
(i,j,\ell+1)
if
k>512
.

Thus if
b_{j}-a_{j}>512
, then the sum of the outputs of the active difference estimators at level
j
is more than
8\cdot 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
In particular since
t_{i,j,a_{j}}\geq c_{j-1}
, the sum of the outputs of the active difference estimators at level
j
after
c_{j-1}
is at least
8\cdot 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v)=2^{-j-2}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
However, by
Lemma
A.11
, each difference estimator at level
j-1
has output at least
2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(u)>8\cdot 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
Specifically, the difference estimator from times
t_{i,j,c_{j-1}}
to
t_{i,j,c_{j}}
must have output at least
2^{-j-7}\cdot F_{p}(u)>8\cdot 2^{-j-8}\cdot F_{p}(v)
.
Therefore, there exists some other
z>c_{j-1}
such that
t_{i,j-1,z}\leq m-W+1
, which contradicts the maximality of
c_{j-1}
at level
j-1
.
∎

By applying the same argument as
Lemma
A.8
using the analogous statements in this section, we have:

Lemma A.14

(Correctness of sliding window algorithm)
.

For
p\in(0,1]
,
Algorithm
9
outputs a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation to
F_{p}(W)
.

Combined,
Lemma
A.14
and
Lemma
A.8
give
Lemma
8.11
.
